# Next targets (2026-10-13)

The open items of [review-2026-10-08.md](review-2026-10-08.md), re-checked on 0ed10be (table-based exceptions
the default), with the items found since. Each was reproduced with a small program built by cppparse
(`--compile6 -O0` and `-O2`, `--msvc-mangling`) and by cl /O2 as the reference.

## Fixed since the review

- 17: a throw inside a catch handler. Every handler body is a try region whose landing pad calls
  `cppparse_catch_unwind` (IrGen.cpp, runtime); correct in table and sjlj mode at -O0 and -O2.
- 18, call side: float arguments of a varargs call are copied into the integer registers (X86.cpp).
- 19, compares and negation: negation widens a float to double first (exact, only slower than cl); compares are
  `ucomiss` on floats since 2026-10-13 (see the floating-point entry below).
- 20, mostly: file-scope `static` functions and variables and the anonymous namespace are internal (the
  namespace carries a per-unit id); an out-of-class `inline` definition is `f i`.
- 22, partly: a missing include is fatal; an undeclared function is error S103.
- 23, partly: a class used before its layout is done and array fields of class type are laid out as cl does.
- 5 (plan 8): empty bases laid out as MSVC does: no space for an empty base, a padding byte between
  zero-sized subobjects, an empty base at the end on the last byte (Sema.cpp layoutClass); test 13 runs
  examples/sample-layout against a cl-compiled driver at -O0..-O3.
- 23 (plan 9): bit-fields laid out as MSVC does (units of the declared type's size, zero-width and unnamed
  bit-fields) and read and written by mask and shift (IrGen loadBits/storeBits); members of anonymous unions
  and structs are named in the enclosing class (Sema declareAnonymousMember). Covered by sample-layout.
- 23 (plan 10): the polymorphic bases are laid out first; the first one is the primary base at offset 0, whose
  vptr the class shares and whose vtable it extends (Sema.cpp layoutClass, completeClass). Covered by
  sample-layout, with virtual calls made by cl on objects built by cppparse and the other way round.
- Lazy instantiation of class template member bodies (MatrixTest.cpp's first blocker).
- 19, stack-passed float parameters: a `float` after the fourth parameter is homed with movss into its 4-byte
  slot, and the copy goes through xmm5, not xmm0 (which still held the first parameter at -O2) (X86.cpp).
- 21, destructors of globals: after a global (or a static local) with a destructor is built, an internal thunk
  that destroys it is registered with atexit, as cl does, so they run after main newest first (IrGen.cpp).
- Destructors that throw (C++98; cl with `/Zc:implicitNoexcept-` is the reference): a destructor's members and
  bases are cleanups of its body (IrGen::destructorParts), so `delete (WB*)new WD` with a throwing `~WD` runs ~WD,
  ~WB, ~WA and the int reaches the handler, and a stack object's throwing destructor is caught; a cleanup's flag is
  cleared before its destructor runs (runCleanup), so it is not destroyed twice; `delete` does not free when the
  destructor throws, as cl. An exception leaving a destructor run by a landing pad calls terminate (handlerCleanups:
  the pad's destructors are a region whose handler calls `cppparse_terminate`, runtime). eh.ps1: ehDtorThrow and
  terminate.cpp, table and sjlj, -O0..-O3.
- 22: an element destructor that throws in `delete[]` (`??_E` and the non-virtual path) or at the end of an array's
  scope: the elements before it are destroyed, newest first, then the exception goes on (cl: 54321, no free); `new
  T[n]` whose k-th constructor throws destroys the k built elements and frees through the matching `operator
  delete[]` (forEachElement's cleanup of the elements [0, index), registerFree). eh.ps1: ehArrays.
- 21, the throwing constructor: `new T` frees through the class's `operator delete` (or the global one, `::new`
  the global one) when the constructor or initializer throws; placement new frees nothing (registerFree). ehArrays.
- 14c, catch by value: `catch (D x)` copy-constructs the handler's object from the thrown one and destroys it when
  the handler ends (also on return, break, continue and a throw out of the handler). eh.ps1: ehCatchValue. A copy
  constructor that throws while it initializes the parameter calls terminate, as cl (terminate-catch.cpp).
- Review of the exception commits: an exception leaving a by-value handler destroys the handler's objects (its
  parameter, then the body's) in the handler's landing pad, before the handled exception is released, as cl; a
  handler for a base that is not the first gets the base subobject (the throw descriptor carries each base's
  offset, `B@16`, which cppparse_match/catch_begin apply); `new P[n]` of a class without destructor and with a
  sized `operator delete[]` frees through it with count * size. Refused with S132 (sample-front/goto-handler.cpp,
  new-refused.cpp): a goto out of a catch handler (accepted since 2026-11-05, row P17: goto-handler.cpp became front.cpp's `gotoh::`), a placement new of a class with its own operator new (called
  since 2026-10-15, row 21), new and
  delete[] of a multidimensional array of a class with its own operator new[] or delete[] (generated since 2026-11-12, row G1). eh.ps1: ehNoCookie,
  ehHandlerOrder, ehCatchBase, terminate-handler.cpp.
- 14b, 14c (2026-10-14): polymorphic virtual bases compile as cl lays them out (the vfptr in the virtual base, a
  class's own vfptr only for new virtual functions, the virtual bases' vftables with adjustor thunks, vtordisp
  fields and cl's `$4` thunks, the vptr and vtordisp stores of constructors and destructors, `??_D`/`??_G`/`??_E`
  from a virtual base's table, dominance in lookup), with cl's names for vbtables of a path of several bases;
  `catch` of a virtual base and a thrown pointer to a class with virtual bases (found through the object's
  vbtable) compile; S130 keeps only the vtordispex case (an override held by another virtual base). Rows 14, 14b,
  14c; test 17 vbase-layout.cpp (50 classes against `/d1reportAllClassLayout`), front.cpp `pvbase::`, test 13
  sample-layout PV..PVN2 and cl's PVE, eh.ps1 ehCatchVbase.
- S3, S4, S5 (lane A, 2026-10-14): a thrown pointer is caught as a pointer to a public unambiguous base (adjusted
  unless null), with more cv-qualifiers on the pointee and as `void*`; a class or pointer is no longer caught as a
  private or ambiguous base (cl does not either); `throw "x"` is caught by `char*` as with cl; throwing a pointer to
  a class with virtual bases was refused (S132: the offset depends on the dynamic type) until 14b, which finds the
  virtual base through the pointed-to object's vbtable. New- and delete-expressions
  call the global operators by cl's names with cl's sizes, so replacements anywhere are called (cl's default
  `operator new` throws `std::bad_alloc` through cl's exception handling, which cppparse frames do not take part
  in). `delete[]` without a cookie calls the class's `operator delete[]`. eh.ps1 ehCatchPointer, ehGlobalFree;
  test 13 sample-layout TR/TQ/TS and the replaced global operators.
- 16 (plan 16): a second polymorphic base gets a secondary vftable (`??_7D@@6BPB@@@`), with MSVC's this adjustors
  and adjustor thunks (`?f@D@@WBA@EAAHXZ`) (Sema.cpp completeClass, IrGen.cpp storeVptr/callFunction/prologue,
  X86.cpp addThunk); calls through every base, by both compilers, are in sample-layout.
- 15, destructor slot: a virtual destructor's slot holds the scalar deleting destructor (`??_G`, IrGen.cpp
  genDeletingDestructors); `delete` calls it with flags 1, so a secondary base pointer frees the complete object.
  It frees through the class-specific `operator delete` lookup finds in the complete class, with its size, and so
  does a non-virtual `delete` (IrGen.cpp freeObject; review repro u.cpp, KD : SA, K).
- A destructor stores its own class's vptrs on entry (IrGen.cpp genFunction), so a virtual call in a base's
  destructor reaches the base's function; sample-layout checks it in constructors and destructors, both compilers.
- 15, vtable slots grouped by overload: a class's new virtual functions are ordered as cl orders them, by
  declaration but with the overloads of a name together where the name is first declared in the class (an
  overrider counts), the last declared first (Sema.cpp completeClass); sample-layout OV/OW and cl's CW : OW, called
  through base pointers by both compilers at -O0..-O3.
- 17, `delete[]` of a polymorphic class across compilers: the destructor slot holds cl's vector deleting destructor
  (`??_E`, COMDAT; flags bit 1: the count from the 8-byte cookie, the elements destroyed last first, freed from the
  cookie through the class's `operator delete[]` with count * size + 8, or the global one), and `delete[]` calls it
  with flags 3, an empty array freed directly, as cl (IrGen.cpp genDeletingDestructors, freeArray, Delete);
  sample-layout AV/AW arrays allocated by each compiler and deleted by the other, empty arrays too.
- 21, class-specific allocation: a new-expression calls the class's own (or inherited) `operator new` / `new[]`
  with cl's size (count * size + 8 with a cookie), and `delete[]` of a class with a cookie frees through its
  `operator delete[]` (count * size + 8); `::new` and `::delete` bypass them (CxxParser.cpp unary,
  Expr::globalScope, IrGen.cpp New/Delete); sample-layout TN/TM, allocated by each compiler and deleted by the other.
- Review of the slot and `??_E` work: the overload grouping counts a non-virtual declaration of the name (ZG); an
  override of a function that a middle class hides is found in the bases' tables, not by name lookup (YC::f(int)
  over YA::f(int) past YB::f(double); before, YC got a new slot and `YA*` calls ran YA::f) (Sema.cpp
  completeClass); a class declaring both `operator delete[](void*)` and the sized form uses the first, as cl, and
  likewise `operator delete` (IrGen.cpp classOperatorDelete); all in sample-layout, both compilers.
- 18, callee side: a variadic function keeps its parameters in their home slots and its prologue spills rcx,
  rdx, r8 and r9 there (named floats from xmm0-xmm3); `include/cstdarg`, `stdarg.h` and `vprintf`/`vfprintf`/
  `vsprintf` in `cstdio` give va_list/va_start/va_arg/va_end over that area, as MSVC's vadefs.h (X86.cpp).
- 20, static locals: cl's names (`?n@?1??counterA@@YAHXZ@4HA`, MangleSema.cpp) and Static linkage, External in
  one COMDAT only for an inline function, so cl and cppparse copies share the variable. A static in a nested
  block gets cl's block number (IrGen::numberStaticBlocks).
- 21, constant tables: a global or static local initialised by constants, string literals and addresses of
  globals and functions (arrays, plain aggregates, pointers) is static data with relocations, in .rdata when
  const, as cl emits it; classes with bit-fields stay on the dynamic path (IrGen.cpp, X86.cpp).
- README "known limit" (untyped library results): with the headers included the version 6 front end types
  `strtod` and `std::string::size`; the paragraph describes the legacy self-compile. Rewritten: the README now
  describes the version 6 behaviour (typed results, S103 and no object without a declaration); test 17 checks both.
- 4 (plan 1): a unit with syntax or semantic errors is not compiled: no object, `.asm` or map (stale ones removed),
  exit code 1 (main.cpp); test 17, `examples/sample-front/errors.cpp`.
- 3 (plan 3): `sizeof(T&)` is `sizeof(T)`; sizeof of a declared-only class, of a class inside its own members, of
  `void` or a function type is S113 (SemaExpr.cpp completeForSizeof); test 17, `sample-front/sizeof-incomplete.cpp`.
- plan 4: variables, parameters and functions are recorded per parser scope; template-name lookup stops at the
  innermost scope that declares the name (CxxParser.cpp isTemplateName); test 17, `sample-front/front.cpp`.
- plan 5: overload candidates are deduplicated by entity before resolution (SemaExpr.cpp resolveOverload), so
  `using namespace std; printf(...)` is no longer ambiguous; test 17, `sample-front/front.cpp`.
- plan 11a: the injected class name with template arguments names that specialisation, as a type and as a
  qualifier (Sema.cpp injectedTemplate); test 17, `sample-front/front.cpp`.
- plan 11b: a function template declared and then defined is one symbol, matched by its written signature; the
  definition keeps the declaration's default arguments (Sema.cpp declareTemplate); test 17, `sample-front/front.cpp`.
- plan 11c: deduction against a template-id in a member template evaluates the arguments that are not its own
  parameters where the template is (the enclosing instantiation's bound R, a literal) and compares them (SemaExpr.cpp
  deduce). MatrixTest.cpp now compiles and prints what cl's build prints at -O0 and -O2 (test 17).
- calls through a function-pointer data member (`e.scale(x)`, `p->scale(x)`, `e.shared(x)` for a static one) called
  an external symbol named after the member: the callee now carries the member's type (SemaExpr.cpp exprCall), so
  the call is indirect through its value; parentheses around the callee are dropped (`(e.scale)(x)`), and `(*fp)(x)`
  and `(*f)(x)` call through the pointer (IrGen.cpp call); test 17, `sample-front/front.cpp`.
- a data member of class type called as a function (`h.f(1)`, `p->f(1)`, `(h.f)(1)`, inherited from a base) called
  operator() with the enclosing object as `this`: it now gets the member's address, converted to operator()'s class
  (IrGen.cpp call); test 17, `sample-front/front.cpp`.
- mapcompare.ps1 (was deferred): cl's functions come from its objects (`dumpbin /symbols`), not from link's map, so
  methods inlined everywhere are listed as "removed by link" in part B (`Matrix::rows()` 3 bytes and `cols()` 4 in
  both compilers), and a part C compares whole classes (`mx::Matrix`: 1.66 times cl's bytes over all its members,
  1.85 over the 17 both emit; cl's link keeps 5 of its 19, ours 13 of 18).
- 22, predefined macros (decided 2026-10-13): the Windows target defines `_MT=1` and `_CPPUNWIND=1`, as cl does for a
  `/MT /EHsc` build (common/Target.cpp); `_MSC_VER` stays undefined, so code under it, written for MSVC's extensions,
  is not compiled; test 17 (`sample-front/front.cpp`) checks the macros against cl.
- Table model, -O2/-O3: a catch inside a loop crashed or lost a value (review repro: `for (k...) { bad = k; try {
  printf("a%d ", 1); thr(1); printf("b%d ", 2); thr(2); } catch (int e) { printf("caught %d\n", e); } }`, access
  violation on the third pass). The runtime was right (`RtlVirtualUnwind` gives each frame its callee-saved
  registers back); the register allocator was not: its liveness had no exception edges, so the inlined throw laid
  out after the loop took rbx, which held the hoisted `"a%d "` that the code after the landing pad still needed.
  The back end now adds an edge from every block whose calls or throws may reach a table landing pad to the
  handlers of that region and of the regions around it (X86.cpp computeEhEdges; liveness and loop extension in
  computeIntervals; Ir.cpp computeLiveness takes the extra edges). eh.ps1: ehCatchLoop, ehNestedLoops
  (probe.cpp), failing at -O2/-O3 before.
- Floating point against cl (review of 2026-10-13, test 17 `fp::` in `sample-front/front.cpp`, -O0 and -O2, also
  checked at -O1 and -O3): double <-> long double was `cvtsd2ss` (IrGen::convert: no conversion now, float <-> long
  double as float <-> double); float compares and conversions to bool used `ucomisd` on a float register and ignored
  PF (`(bool)-0.0f` true, `(bool)NaN` false, `NaN == NaN` true): `ucomiss`/`ucomisd` by the operands' type, and
  ==/!= test PF in the setcc (a `jp` around it) and in the fused branch (X86.cpp emitInst Cmp/CondBr, invertCc);
  `x + 0.0` was folded to `x` (-0.0 gave -0.0): only x*1, x/1, x-(+0.0), x+(-0.0) are kept (Optimizer::algebraic);
  a constant's text, the CSE/GVN key, had 6 digits (`x+0.1` and `x+0.1000001` were one value) and the phi
  simplification took -0.0 for 0.0 (IrOperand::str, Ssa phi); `float op float` was a double (TypeTable::
  arithmeticResult promoted float, and unary -/+ did too: `sizeof(-f)` 8): it is a float computed with addss etc.,
  `-f` is negated through a double (the back end's sign mask is a double's), and folded float operations, float
  operands and conversions are rounded to float (Optimizer::constantFold).

## Silent differences found by the reviews of 2026-10-14

Two reviews re-checked the review fixes of 2026-10-13 and the gap list on d9309fa, each case built by cppparse
(-O0, -O2, table and sjlj) and by cl (/Od, /O2, `/Zc:implicitNoexcept-` for throwing destructors). The review
fixes themselves held (catch of a non-first base, handler order, no-cookie frees, the S132 refusals, the
virtual-base fixes, C2584/C4584). These are wrong results with no diagnostic, none recorded before:

| # | Case | Repro | cl | cppparse | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | value-initialisation `T()` of a class without a user constructor | `struct Q { int* f; int x; }; Q q = Q();` (also `Q q((Q()))`, `return Q();`, `q = Q();`, `new Q()`) | all zero | fixed (was stack garbage at -O0 and -O2): zeroed, then the implicit constructor when it has work (members with constructors, a vptr, vbptrs); a user constructor alone; also `new T[n]()`, `D() : B(), m(), arr()` (a base: its own bytes), `T()` of a template parameter; `new Q` stays uninitialised. Test 17 front.cpp `valinit::` | IrGen::valueInit, called by materialize, value (FunctionalCast), New, constructorInit |
| S2 | a `float` argument of a variadic function | `float f = third(); printf("%.9g", f);` | 0.333333343 | fixed (was 5.19e-315 at -O0, 0.333333312 at -O2): sema wraps every argument matching `...` in a cast to its promoted type (float -> double, bool/char/short/wchar_t/enums -> int), for calls by name, through pointers and to constructors; the folded constant is converted exactly; va_arg reads the promoted types. Test 17 front.cpp `varargs::` | SemaExpr Sema::promoteVariadic (resolveOverload, exprCall) |
| S3 | `catch (B*)` of a thrown `D*` | `struct XP { int a; }; struct HB { int b; }; struct MB : XP, HB {}; MB m; try { throw &m; } catch (HB* p) {}` | handler taken, `p` adjusted to the HB subobject | was `catch (...)`, or terminate; fixed (lane A): the same | fixed: IrGen throwDescriptorFor/catchableBases list a pointer's public unambiguous bases (`HB*+4`) and `void*`, runtime cppparse_match/catch_begin check the pointee's cv-qualifiers and adjust a non-null pointer in a slot behind the copy; sample-eh ehCatchPointer |
| S4 | replaced global `operator new` / `delete` / `new[]` / `delete[]` | `void* operator new(size_t n) { ++g; return malloc(n); }`, then `new int(5)` | called | was ignored (`__new`/`__delete` went to malloc/free); fixed (lane A): called | fixed: X86 runtimeName gives cl's names (`??2@YAPEAX_K@Z`, `??_U@YAPEAX_K@Z`, sized `??3@YAXPEAX_K@Z` always, `??_V@YAXPEAX_K@Z` with a cookie and on throwing-constructor paths, `??_V@YAXPEAX@Z` without), a definition of one gets that name in either mangling (replacedAllocationName); IrGen freeGlobal/freeArray pass cl's sizes; exception objects stay malloc (`__malloc`); sample-layout (replacements in the cl driver and in layout.cpp), sample-eh ehGlobalFree |
| S5 | `delete[]` of a class without cookie and with its own `operator delete[]` | `struct R { static void operator delete[](void* p) { log(); free(p); } }; delete[] new R[1];` (also the sized form) | R's operator (the sized form gets one element's size: the count is unknown) | was the global one; fixed (lane A): R's | fixed: IrGen Delete calls freeArray without a cookie (count unknown); sample-layout TR/TQ/TS, deleted by each compiler |
| S6 | multicharacter constants | `'ab'`, `'abcd'` | 24930, 1633837924 | 97, 97 | fixed: common/Literal.cpp charLiteralValue (SemaExpr CharLit, Pp `#if`): an int, each character shifted in from the right until the second escape sequence, from there on each put above the ones before, as cl (`'\x12\x34'` 3412h, `'a\n\tb'` 6209610Ah); front.cpp `strlit` |
| S7 | a hex escape before a concatenated literal | `"\x41" "b"` | "Ab", strlen 2 | one char 0x1B, strlen 1 | fixed: CxxParser keeps the pieces as written, common/Literal.cpp stringLiteralUnits decodes each piece on its own for sema (sizes), the X86 string table and char-array initialisers (`"\0" "1"` was `\01`); wide literals were emitted as the bytes of their text (`L"ab"`) and are now 16-bit units (X86 `DW`, `wchar_t w[] = L".."` locals and static data); `char s[] = ".."` statics are static data with no guard, as cl; front.cpp `strlit` |
| S8 | out-of-range floating constants in static initialisers (undefined, cl keeps a value) | `static double v = (unsigned char)300.0 + 0.5;`, `static int n = (int)-2147483649.0;` | 300.5, 2147483647 | 44.5, -2147483648 | fixed: cl's rule (checked against cl /Od and /O2, code and data): a floating constant converted to an integer is truncated, taken modulo 2^64 and narrowed (common/Literal.cpp foldFloatToInteger); converted straight on to a floating type (a cast, an initialiser, an argument, the usual arithmetic conversions; through parentheses and a unary + that promotes nothing) it keeps that untruncated 64-bit value: `(unsigned char)300.0 + 0.5` is 300.5, `(double)(int)3e9` 3e9, while `-`, `?:`, integral conversions and arithmetic work on the narrowed value. IrGen::foldArith/foldToFloating/foldInteger/convertFolded; 64-bit values beyond 32 bits go out as two DD items; sample-statics konstF (guards as cl), front.cpp `cfold` |
| S9 | literal casts with a non-literal constant operand | `(int)-2147483649.0`, `(int)+3e9`, `(int)(2.0 * 1.5e9)`, `(int)(float)1e10`, `const double cd = 3e9; (int)cd` | folded modulo 2^N (7fffffff, b2d05e00, ...) | 80000000h | fixed: value() folds a floating constant expression (literals, + - * /, casts, ?: with a constant or compared condition, const variables with a constant initializer) to one FConst, and IrGen::convert folds the conversion of an FConst to an integer and keeps the raw value for a floating conversion that follows at once; sema folds the same casts into integral constants (Sema::floatingConstant, exprCast; `(unsigned long long)(unsigned)-1.0` is no longer taken as 2^64-1); front.cpp `cfold` |
| S10 | member-pointer size of a class completed later in the unit | `struct Inc; int a = sizeof(int Inc::*); struct Inc { int x; };` | 12 (24 for a function) | fixed (was 4 (8)): cl fixes the model where it first needs the size (sizeof, a member's layout) and keeps the unspecified one once the class is defined; naming the type (a typedef, a parameter) decides nothing (`typedef void (M::*MF)();` before `struct M : A, B` is 16 in cl). Sema marks the class (Symbol::memberPointerUnspecified) when TypeTable::sizeOf meets it incomplete, and for the bodies and initializers analysed after the declarations when sizeof names a class defined after them (Symbol::defOrder; a member function defined in its class counts where the outermost class ends). Test 17 front.cpp `mplater::` | Sema.cpp TypeTable::sizeOf, Sema::memberPointerSizeUse, queueBody, declareClass |
| S11 | a namespace-scope object of a template with a member-pointer field | `template <class T> struct H { int T::* f; }; H<P> gh;` | f = -1 | fixed with row 18 (was 0, then refused): static storage holds the null values where cl writes them (`H<P> gh` -1, `Outer go[2]` and `Two gtwo` 0), value-initialisation zeroes as cl does, a brace list's omitted pointers to members are null (`H<P> h = {}` -1, `Outer o = {}` 0). Test 17 front.cpp `memptr::` | IrGen::nullMemberPointerData, nullMemberPointers, memberPointerData |
| S12 | the halfway float literal (was filed as mixed links only) | `1.00000005960464477550f` as a global, local or static | 0x3F800001 | 0x3F800000 | fixed: common/Literal.cpp floatingLiteralValue rounds once (when the double is exactly halfway between two floats, the decimal is compared exactly with that midpoint), used by sema, IrGen value and foldArith; such a static is static data with no guard, as cl; front.cpp `cfold`, sample-statics konstF |

Also found, errors on valid code (not wrong code): `int a[(int)(unsigned char)300.0 + 1];` was S114 (a floating
literal cast to an integral type is an integral constant expression; fixed with S9, also `(int)(2.0 * 1.5)` and
`(int)cd` of a const double as cl takes them); a string literal initialising a char array member in braces,
`struct N { char n[6]; int v; } g = { "ab", 3 };`, is S105 "cannot initialise an element of type char from const char*"
(Sema::braceElement; IrGen::initAggregate would need the literal's units too); an explicit template argument on a call
`run<PS>(3)` is S107; `n(sv)` with `S::operator int()` and overloads `n(int)`, `n(long)` is S106 ambiguous (cl
picks `n(int)`). Code size at d9309fa (mapcompare's counting on the matrix sample, -O2): the 17 Matrix members
both emit 1.79 of cl's bytes, the whole class 1.60, all 95 common functions 1.55, the ten largest 3.02.

## Review of 2026-10-15 (sandbox\out\fable-review\REPORT.md): the 35 gaps and their state

An independent review of a6a53c2 built 60 programs with cl and cppparse and ranked 35 gaps (blockers B1-B6, major
M1-M15, minor m1-m10); its section 5 gives the order of work followed here. Every fix is compared against cl at
-O0 and -O2 (test 17 front.cpp, sample-lib, sample-eh) and went through the full suite with the selfhost bootstrap:
before each commit cppparse compiles its own sources, the second-generation compiler compiles them again, and the
two generations must be identical.

Work stopped on 2026-10-15 after M14 (the user's week); Fable 5.1 finished the rest on 2026-10-20/21 in the order
the brief gave, each item with its regression test, the full suite and the self-hosting check green before its
commit: M11 (c8a1785, `char*` from a string literal), m4 (13376c8, `_purecall` in the pure virtual slots), m6
(83d727b, the COFF writer's errors name the function and quote the line, the driver calls them internal errors),
m2 (6bb48fa, the driver's `-q`, exit code, response files and output names), the m8/m9 measurements (de48343),
M10 (8e4d3c2, CodeView line tables read back through dbghelp), and the won't-fix records below re-checked at the
end. Found on the way by the M11 checks of the review's misc1.cpp and misc3.cpp and fixed after the list (see
"Other known limits"): a user-defined `operator,` was bypassed, and the address of an `extern "C"` function was
referenced as a variable (`g_cfunc`).

| # | Gap | State |
|---|---|---|

the review's stdlib.cpp, stdlib_min.cpp, classes.cpp, lib2.cpp print what cl prints): new headers `<climits> <cfloat> <limits>` (numeric_limits for every fundamental type, MSVC's values; the infinities and NaNs are folded constants), `<cerrno> <ciso646> <csignal> <csetjmp> <clocale> <cwchar> <cwctype>` (MSVC's C runtime), `<__iterator>` (categories, iterator_traits, reverse_iterator, advance, distance; the containers include it) and `<iterator>` (the insert and stream iterators), `<functional>` (the functors, negators, binders, ptr_fun, mem_fun, mem_fun_ref; the comparison functors moved here from `<algorithm>`), `<numeric>`, `<memory>` (allocator, auto_ptr, uninitialized_*), `<deque>` (a contiguous block with room at both ends: pointer iterators, push_front and push_back may reallocate), `<list>` (a doubly linked list with splice, merge, sort, unique, remove, reverse), `<stack> <queue>` (queue and priority_queue over the heap algorithms), `<bitset>`, `<iomanip>` (setw, setfill, setprecision, setbase, setiosflags, resetiosflags on both stream kinds); `<algorithm>` rewritten over any iterator with the C++98 set (for_each, find_*, count_*, equal, search, copy_backward, swap_ranges, transform, replace_*, generate, remove_*, unique_copy, reverse_copy, rotate, random_shuffle, partition, stable_partition, sort, stable_sort, partial_sort, nth_element, lower/upper_bound, equal_range, binary_search, merge, inplace_merge, includes, the set operations, the heap operations, min/max_element, lexicographical_compare, next/prev_permutation); `vector`, `string`, `deque` and `list` have rbegin/rend; `vector::at` throws out_of_range; `<new>` has nothrow_t, nothrow and the nothrow forms (malloc: null on failure, since the C runtime's operator new reports failure through MSVC's exception handling); `<exception>` has set_terminate, terminate, set_unexpected, unexpected and uncaught_exception over the runtime (cppparse_set_terminate etc.; the landing pads count the destructors they run, so uncaught_exception() is true while an exception unwinds; an unhandled exception now calls the terminate handler); `cerr` and `cin` are tied to `cout` (ios::tie); `-0.0` prints `-0`. Compiler work the headers needed: deduction from member pointer types (`mem_fun(R (T::*)() const)`) and the const of a member function pointer, a concrete parameter type deduces nothing (`__distance(..., input_iterator_tag)` takes a derived tag), a placement new whose arguments are not one address calls the operator new it resolves (the class's own first: `new (arena) T` with `T::operator new(size_t, Arena&)`, `new (std::nothrow) T`), the module initialiser is internal (two units whose first dynamic global comes from one header defined the same `__cppparse_init_` name). Left: `std::string` is not `basic_string` (no `wstring`), `map::value_type` is `pair<K, V>`, `string::at` aborts instead of throwing, `<complex> <valarray> <locale> <streambuf> <strstream>` are not provided, a sliced `catch (std::exception e)` loses the message (cl keeps it through an MSVC extension), a placement new of an array with arguments other than an address is refused (S132) |

the estimate stands. 2026-11-18: in work after the go-ahead; the design, with its independent review folded in (section 10) and the stages 0 to 5, is docs/design-cl-exceptions.md; the exception code moves to its own folder cppparse/eh/ first (stage 0, done 2026-11-18: the table and setjmp models' code as classes there, every output byte for byte as before; stage 0b done: an exception that crosses between cppparse's models and cl's frames now stops with a message of the runtime where it was passed on silently, eh.ps1's new section ehx.ps1, examples/sample-ehx; stage 1 done: `--eh=cl` throws through `_CxxThrowException` with a ThrowInfo of its own per thrown type (eh/EhClThrowInfo), 27 kinds of thrown type are taken by cl's handlers as cl's own throws are, at -O0..-O3; stage 2 done: a frame's cleanups are states with funclets (eh/EhClFunclets, eh/EhClFuncInfo with its checker of the tables), so a cl exception that passes a cppparse `--eh=cl` frame destroys its objects in cl's order: sample-ehx `unwind`; stage 3 done: handlers are funclets with HandlerTypes (IrGen::tryStatementCl), so cl's throws are caught by cppparse's handlers and cppparse's by cl's, by value, reference and pointer: eh.ps1's probe prints cl's 89 lines in the model at -O0..-O3, sample-ehx `catch` and `cross` equal the all-cl build with either side or both from cppparse, and the compiler builds itself in the model with generation two identical at -O0 and -O2; stage 4 done: the terminate handler and uncaught_exception are the C runtime's in every model, objects carry /FAILIFMISMATCH so link refuses `cl` beside `table`/`sjlj`, sample-ehx `state` and the /MD, /DEBUG /INCREMENTAL, /OPT:REF,ICF and one-library links pass, and failnew.ps1's injected allocation failures unwind the self-built compiler in both models: 120 runs, each ends with "out of memory"; stage 5 done, M7 complete: measured against the table model, cl's model has equal code (within 1 %) and equal spills but more unwind data (sum of sections: matrix +6.2 %, sample-lib +6.1 %, own sources +7.5 % at -O0 and +2.5 % at -O2), the matrix time is equal, a throw through ten frames takes 5.9 us against the table model's 7.1 and cl's 5.4, the path without a throw 45 ns against 40 and 35; the 3 % size criterion does not hold, so the default stays `table` and `--eh=cl` is the option for programs with cl's code. Left as rows of their own: FH4 tables, one unwind entry for a function's funclets, the table model's cost of a throw in a function of many regions, cause not looked into (a throw out of a frame with 1,000 live objects takes 3.1 ms in the table model, 0.10 ms in cl's, 0.06 ms in cl's own code). The design's findings: (measured: when only the frames between thrower and catcher are the other compiler's, their destructors and handlers are skipped without a message; recommended: a third model `--eh=cl` that emits cl's own tables and funclets for `__CxxFrameHandler3` and throws with `_CxxThrowException`, in five stages) |

The parser reports `int f(int a {` as a missing `)` after the parameter list, a broken initializer as the declaration's error, and recovery stops after a balanced block so the statements after a bad `for` header are parsed (cl's C2143/C2059 lines). Not done: C4701 (potentially uninitialised), C4458 (a local hiding a member), C4459 (hiding a global), the note lines cl adds. Found on the way by the new shadowing warning: a member function named like a type of an enclosing scope (bitset::set with `<set>` included) made `set(i)` in a member body a declaration of `i` of type `std::set`, silently dropping the call (the string constructor of bitset built an empty set); the parser now scans a class body for its member functions' names before parsing the members, and a function, variable or parameter of an inner scope hides a type of the same name further out (front.cpp `membername::`) |

| M14 | `m->v` with `Mid::operator->` returning `Inner&` and `Inner::operator->` returning `Inner*`: S117 | fixed: operator-> is applied again while the result is a class with one (13.5.6), up to 32 times. front.cpp `arrow::` against cl; the review's misc3.cpp line compiles |
| M11 | `char* p = "legacy";` and `useLit("abc")` with `int useLit(char*)`: S105 / S107 | fixed (2026-10-20): a string literal converts to `char*` (`wchar_t*` for `L"..."`), the deprecated conversion of 4.2/2 (Sema::convert: only for a literal, possibly parenthesised, and only to a pointer to the non-const element type), ranked as an array-to-pointer conversion followed by a qualification conversion, so `f(const char*)` is chosen over `f(char*)` as cl does; in initialisations, assignments, arguments, returns and constructor arguments. front.cpp `charlit::` against cl at -O0 and -O2; the review's misc1.cpp `legacy` line and misc3.cpp `useLit("abc")` print what cl prints |
| m2 | the driver: `-q` prints the statistics, `-o` to a missing directory exits 0, a response file needs one token per line, two inputs are named `two_a.cpp-06.obj` | fixed (2026-10-20): `-q` prints only diagnostics (the parser's, the preprocessor's and sema's errors and warnings; no statistics, no "wrote" lines, no "not compiled" summary); an output that cannot be written is reported and makes the exit code 1 (writeOut counts the failures); a response file holds white-space separated arguments, any number per line, a path with spaces in double quotes, a line starting with `#` a comment (as cl's: the old one-token-per-line files still read, unquoted paths with spaces in them no longer); the version 6 outputs of an input are named after its stem (`two_a.cpp` gives `two_a6.obj`), `-n` with several inputs numbers them (`name-0`, `name-1`). Test 13 checks the four, README and --help describe them |
| m3 | iostream details | fixed in B6 (`-0`, `cerr.tie()`); a sliced `catch (std::exception e)` loses the message: C++98's `std::exception` has no message, cl keeps it through an MSVC extension (a `what()` pointer in the base); not done |
| m4 | a pure virtual function called from a constructor: segfault | fixed (2026-10-20): the vftable slot of a pure virtual function holds vcruntime's `_purecall` (R6025 "pure virtual function call", a fail-fast with exit code 0xC0000409) as cl's tables do, instead of 0 (IrGen registerMetadata, X86 emitVtables). eh.ps1 purecall.cpp: output and exit code as cl's build, table and sjlj, -O0 and -O2. The call goes through a volatile function pointer: a plain `f()` in the constructor is devirtualised by cl, which then wants a body for the pure function (LNK2019; the review's fuzz/purecall.cpp does not link with cl) |
| m6 | an internal error names an assembly line, not the source | improved (2026-10-20): the COFF writer's message names the function (or the data item) the line belongs to and quotes the line (`line 168 in ?f@@YAXH@Z: lea: float register used with an integer operand [lea rax, xmm0]`), and the driver calls it an internal error of cppparse (the code generator produced assembly the COFF writer cannot encode), asks for a report with the unit and points at the `<name>6.asm` listing. No unit triggers it today (the review's conv1.cpp compiles since B3), so `--compile6 --obj <name>.asm` assembles a listing of cppparse's MASM subset through the writer alone (a way to rebuild an object from an edited listing as well) and test 13 feeds it a listing with a bad line and a good one. A source line is not named: the assembly carries none (M10's `;@line` markers will carry the IR's) |
| m8, m9 | compile speed, optimisation quality | measured (2026-10-20 on 6bb48fa, the compiler after m2 and before M10's CodeView tables; the median of three runs on the review's machine; the review's numbers in brackets). m8, the review's big.cpp (6047 lines, 2000 classes, 12128 functions after instantiation): `--compile6 --obj` 17.5 s at -O0 (19.7 s) and 29.0 s at -O2 (28.0 s), cl /Od 3.2 s (3.9 s), cl /O2 /bigobj 63.3 s; the objects 11.7 MB at -O0 and 11.3 MB at -O2 against cl's 11.8 MB (/Od) and 14.1 MB (/O2 /bigobj). About 5.5 times cl /Od's time at -O0, the -O2 passes adding two thirds; not worked on. m9, the review's bench.cpp, ms at cppparse -O2 / cl /O2: sieve 222 / 189 (331 / 329), 300x300 matmul 56 / 11 (61 / 12), FNV hash 161 / 107 (167 / 108), virtual-call loop 222 / 186 (234 / 188), Vec3 by value 435 / 62 (443 / 63); at cppparse -O0 / cl /Od: 297 / 287, 98 / 92, 345 / 312, 406 / 372, 1250 / 1346. Code of all its functions: 5071 bytes at -O2 against cl /O2's 3603 (1.41x; the review 1.39x), 5834 at -O0 against cl /Od's 3254. So -O2 stays between cl /Od and cl /O2: within 20% of cl /O2 on the sieve, the hash and the virtual calls, 5x on matmul (no vectorisation, no unrolling) and 7x on the class-temporary loop (the plan's rows A, B, E below). The optimisation work follows the plan, not the review |
| m5 (won't fix) | dynamic exception specifications are ignored | as cl, re-checked 2026-10-21: cl ignores them too (C4290 "C++ exception specification ignored"); `throw(int)` on a function that throws a double is parsed and dropped, `std::unexpected` and `set_unexpected` exist (B6) but nothing calls unexpected(), as nothing does in cl's programs. Also as cl: an exception no handler takes calls the terminate handler; unlike cl, the destructors of the frames in between have run by then (the review's ehspec.cpp shows `~X` before `terminate called`), since the landing pads cannot be told from handlers before the unwinding starts; a program must not rely on the state of its objects inside terminate() either way. Stays so |
| m7 (won't fix) | `export` | re-checked 2026-10-21: `export template <class T> T ident(T x)` is a parse error, as it is for cl (C3348 "exported templates are not part of the current C++ standards": the review's misc3.cpp does not build with cl either); no mainstream compiler ever implemented it and C++11 removed it. Stays a parse error |
| m10 (won't fix) | other targets | re-checked 2026-10-21: x86_64 Windows is the only target with a back end; `--target x86_64-linux` gives LP64 sizes and macros to -E, --parse6 and --sema6, and --compile6 refuses it with exit code 2 ("no back end for target"); no 32-bit target. Out of scope: the ABI work (layout, vftables, unwind tables, CodeView) is MSVC's by design |
| M15 (as documented) | the listed refusals and silent differences | re-checked 2026-10-21, unchanged: the refusals S130 (an override held by another virtual base), S131 (covariant returns through a virtual base or with a this adjustment), S132 (member-pointer conversions into a class that does not share the vbptr, a cast from a virtual base down, placement new of an array with arguments); a pointer to member as a template argument (S107); `case 1LL << 40` and the other 64-bit constant expressions (S114; Expr::semaValue is a 32-bit long); the arguments of a call evaluated left to right (cl right to left) and NRVO only for one local directly in the body; all as the docs above list them, none silent beyond the evaluation order |

## Triage of 2026-10-21 (Fable 5.1: what is left after the review, in the order of work)

Every "Left:" of the tables above and of "Other known limits" re-checked on f644463 with a probe built by cl (/Od, /O2)
and by cppparse (-O0, -O2), plus the common C++98 template shapes none of the reviews had tried (found refused on the
way: out-of-class definitions of a class template's constructors, member templates and nested classes, a static const
member as an array bound). The order: silent differences first, then valid code refused, the library, then the records.
Done the same day, one commit each with its regression test, the full suite and the self-hosting bootstrap (39 of 39
units identical, generation two identical) green before every commit: A1-A4, B1-B6, B8, B13, C1, C2; the rest is
recorded below with its reason (next work or won't fix). The probes live in the session's scratch folder; what they
showed is in each row.

(a) Silent differences from cl (wrong code, no diagnostic):

| # | Case | State |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | the dynamic initialiser of a class template's static data member never runs: `template <class T> Log Box<T>::made(7);` leaves `made` zero (and runs `~Log` at exit), `template <class T> int Box<T>::dyn = side();` gives 0, in one unit as in two (declareTemplate ignored the definition; B5's "Left": every unit ran its own initialiser) | fixed (2026-10-21): the definition is kept on the class template and declared with each instantiation (Sema::declareTemplateMemberDefinition, also for the instantiations made before it), where its initialiser is analysed; a COMDAT variable with a dynamic initialiser gets cl's COMDAT initializer function `??__E?made@?$Box@H@@2ULog@@A@@YAXXZ` and a `.CRT$XCU` entry `??made$initializer$@?$Box@H@@2P6AXXZEA@@3P6AXXZEA` in a COMDAT associative with the variable's own section, so the linker keeps one initialiser per program and /OPT:REF keeps it with the variable (IrGen::registerMetadata/genInitializer/initGlobal, X86Module::addComdatInitializer, Coff.cpp `;@comdat assoc <symbol>`, MangleSema initializerEntry); the same names as cl's, so a mixed link runs it once too. sample-lib lib1/lib2 linkageA/linkageB (LinkDyn<int>::name, text, viaCall built once across both units, test 15 and lib.ps1) |
| A2 | `new (addr) T[n]` ignores the placement address: the array is allocated from the heap (classes and scalars alike) | fixed (2026-10-21): the elements are built at the given address, with no cookie (as cl: such an array is destroyed by hand), an element's throwing constructor destroys the ones built and frees nothing (IrGen New, the array branch). front.cpp `placement::` (a class with a destructor and ints placed in a buffer, the addresses and counts as cl's at -O0 and -O2) |
| A3 | the matching placement `operator delete` is not called when the constructor of a placement new throws (5.3.4/17; `new (arena) T(-1)` with `T::operator delete(void*, Arena&)`, `new (5) U(-2)` with a global `operator delete(void*, int)`) | fixed (2026-10-21): the operator new sema resolved is called with its arguments evaluated once into locals (IrGen::placementNew), and a cleanup calls the placement operator delete of the same scope with the same parameter types after the first (5.3.4/19; IrGen::matchingPlacementDelete, Cleanup freeKind 3) with the saved arguments when the constructor throws; the class's own first, else the global one; a class argument by value keeps the old path (nothing freed). front.cpp `placement::` (Thrower with its own Arena forms, Boom through the global ones, the counts and arena state as cl's at -O0 and -O2) |
| A4 | `typeid(int[3]).name()` is `int * __ptr64 const` (cl `int [3]`): the type descriptor used the mangler's parameter form of an array | fixed (2026-10-21): the descriptor spells an array as cl does, `.$$BY02H` (the rank, each dimension, the element; `??_R0$$BY02H@8`), so name() undecorates to `int [3]` and `typeid(int[3]) != typeid(int[4])` (MangleSema::rttiTypeName). front.cpp `rtti::` (arrays of ints, classes, pointers, three dimensions) |
| A5 | the arguments of a call are evaluated left to right; cl evaluates them right to left and the object expression of a member call (`getO(o).m(n(7), n(8))`) after the arguments, and the right operand of `=` before the left (`arr[n(0)] = n(4)`) | fixed (2026-10-23); first recorded (2026-10-21): unspecified in C++98 (5/4, 5.2.2/8), so no program may rely on it, and cl's scheme is three rules, not one (right to left for the arguments, the object expression of a member call after them, the right operand of an assignment first); the earlier attempt (row 14d (4)) matched cl on the arguments alone and broke the bootstrap, which the second and third rule explain as well as an order-dependent call in the own sources would (the cl-built generation 1 evaluates `this` last, the self-built one first). Taking it means all three rules at once, then the bootstrap diff tells which own-source call depended on the order. Next work, after the correctness items. Re-checked (2026-10-23), not done: measured with a probe of n(v) calls that log their order, cl's scheme is the same at /Od and /O2: the arguments right to left (`three(n(1), n(2), n(3))`: 3 2 1, five arguments too, class temporaries as well: `takeC(C(11), C(12))` builds C(12) first), a call nested in an argument evaluated as part of that argument (`three(n(1), three(n(2), n(3), n(4)), n(5))`: 5 4 3 2 1), the object expression of a member call after the arguments (`getO(9).m(n(7), n(8))`: 8 7 9), the right operand of `=` before the left (`arr[n(0)] = n(4)`: 4 0), and binary operators left to right (`n(1) + n(2) * n(3)`: 1 2 3); cppparse gives 1 2 3, 9 7 8, 0 4 at both levels. So one scheme would match both levels; the places are IrGen::callFunction (the argument loop), the member call path that evaluates the object before callFunction (IrGen.cpp Expr::Call) and the assignment lowering (Expr::Assign in value() and lvalue()). Left for a session of its own: it touches every call and assignment of every unit, and the own sources' order dependences (bitConstant's "one constant per statement" shows the kind) must be found with the bootstrap diff, with copies.cpp's logs against cl as the check Done (2026-10-23), measured again with four probes of logging calls at /Od and /O2 (order2-order5 in the session's scratch folder), where cl's scheme turned out to have a fourth rule: an argument that only reads (a variable, a member through a pointer, arithmetic or a cast on them) is loaded last, right before the call, after the arguments that call a function or have a side effect, and after the object of a member call (`show2(set(t, 5), t)` passes 5 5, `getS(13).m2(t, set(t, 14))` passes the t getS set), and within a binary operator a read operand is loaded after an operand that calls (`t + set(t, 10)` is 20). The own sources depend on that rule: IrGen's `toBool(conditionDecl(s->decl, t, line), t, line)` reads the t conditionDecl set, so the first attempt, right to left alone, made generation two differ from generation one in four units (CxxParser, Sema, SemaExpr, IrGen: a condition declaration's pointer tested without its comparison), which the bootstrap diff pointed at. IrGen now evaluates as cl: callArguments evaluates the arguments in two phases (those that call, construct, copy, convert through a function or have a side effect, right to left, a default argument first; then, after the object of a member call, the function pointer of an indirect call or the object and pointer of a `.*` call, the arguments that only read; IrGen::readsOnly, pureExpr), for direct, member, operator, indirect, pointer-to-member and constructor calls and for a placement new's arguments (emitCall builds the call once this is known; operatorCall evaluates a member operator's object after its argument); a built-in `=` evaluates its right operand first, a compound assignment its left one (builtinAssign, which lvalue() of an assignment now uses instead of evaluating the left operand twice); a built-in binary operator loads a read operand after an operand that calls. cppparse now prints what cl /Od and cl /O2 print for every case of the probes but two, where cl /Od and /O2 themselves differ or cl's choice is its own: an assignment as an argument (`show2(t = 3, set(t, 4))`: cl performs the assignment before the call to its right and reads t afterwards, [4 4]; cppparse [3 4]), and two increments of one variable as arguments (`show2(t++, t++)`: cl [0 0], cppparse [1 0], undefined behaviour anyway, 5/4); a comparison or a conditional with a read operand in an argument beside a call that changes it is evaluated early by cl /Od and late by cl /O2 (`show2(pick(t != 0), set(t, 15))` [0 15] at /Od, [1 15] at /O2), cppparse follows /O2 at both levels, as the bootstrap requires (generation one is cl /O2's code). Found with the probes and fixed: a class's operator new and operator delete not declared static were taken for non-static members (12.5/1, 12.5/6: they are static), so `new (31) O(32, 33)` with `void* operator new(size_t, int)` passed the size as this and corrupted the heap. front.cpp `evalorder::` (arguments of direct, nested, default, variadic, member, chained member, indirect and operator calls, class temporaries by value and by reference, constructors, built-in assignments and compound assignments, the read-last rule) against cl at -O0 and -O2; copies.cpp unchanged and still as cl's. The self-built compiler's multi-threaded `--health` now dies on nearly every run of the race in C3's `<__locale>` (the reference counts are not interlocked; the library side is fixing it): with A5 the analyzer's threads hit it far more often than the 1 run in 8 measured on the base; over a copy of `<__locale>` with interlocked counts the self-built compiler completes 10 runs of 10 and prints what the cl-built one prints, multi-threaded and with -j 1 |
| A6 | (found by C3's tests) a class object passed to a `const T&` parameter of another class T with a converting constructor from it is bound to the object's own bytes, no T is made: `struct Arr { Arr(const View&); }; void show(const Arr&);` then `show(View(1, 2))` or `show(v)` reads the View as an Arr (a local `const Arr& r = View(1, 2);` and `Arr b = v;` are right) | fixed (2026-10-23): IrGen::argument took a class operand of another class for a temporary of the parameter's class and passed its address; an object converted by a converting constructor of the target is now built into a temporary of the target (materialize), the reference bound to it and the temporary destroyed at the end of the full-expression (8.5.3/5, 12.2/3), as for a local reference. materialize chooses the constructor taking the operand's own type first, then one taking a base of the operand's class, then one taking an arithmetic type or a pointer, never an explicit one, and the specialisations sema made of a constructor template are among them (a Der given to `Arr(const Base&)` found no constructor before and left the temporary unbuilt, by value too; `m.insert(std::make_pair(std::string("c"), 3))` passed the pair<string, int> as map's pair<const string, int>, right only because the two layouts agree). Found with it and fixed: `const Arr& r = View(10, 11);` kept the View alive with the reference (every temporary of the initializer became the block's); only the bound Arr lives on now, the View is destroyed at the end of the full-expression as cl does (12.2/5). front.cpp `convarg::` (a View prvalue and lvalue, a derived class through `Arr(const Base&)`, by value, a second parameter, a constructor, a member function, a member and a free operator, references bound to converted temporaries, every construction and destruction logged) against cl at -O0 and -O2; sample-lib valarray.cpp passes indirect_array, gslice_array, mask_array and slice_array straight to a `const valarray&` (test 15). A call with several converted arguments builds them left to right, cl right to left (row A5) |
| A7 | (found by C3's tests) a member operator() template called on an object (`struct F { template <class T> int operator()(T, T) const; }; F f; f(1, 2)`) is called without the object: no `this` (a crash at run time) | fixed (2026-10-23): IrGen::call told the object call by `fn->name == "operator()"`, and a specialisation of a member template is named `operator()<int>`; the call operator is now also the specialisation of a template named operator() (fn->instantiatedFrom), so the object is passed as this for `f(1, 2)` on an object, a reference, `(*pf)(8, 9)`, a data member (`h.g(6)`), a temporary (`F()(4, 4)`) and inside a function template (`fn(a, b)` with `const Fn&`); an explicit `f.operator()(2, 3)` took the member path already. front.cpp `optmpl::` (const and non-const templates beside a plain overload, deduced from int and double, one that changes its object) against cl at -O0 and -O2. `<locale>`'s plain operator() members for string and wstring beside its template are no longer needed (the library side's to drop) |
