Found with the names and fixed: a vftable of a class with several was named for the class that introduced its vfptr; cl names it for the base it was inherited through at the level where two tables would first be named alike (D : A, M with M : B: ??_7D@@6BM@@@, not 6BB@@@; F : A, W with W : virtual V: 6BW@@@, not 6BV@@@), now computed as MSVC 2012's scheme (IrGen::vfptrPaths; SemaMangler::vtable takes the path), the vftables and their RTTI locators alike. front.cpp `covvbt::` (CV, CW without a vtordisp, CV2, CV3, OV, DV with VB elsewhere than in a complete CV, a null result; through VB*, CV* and directly) and `mpdownt::` (data and function members down, null, the unspecified model up, down, through offset 0 and from a virtual base's member, applied to both classes) against cl at -O0 and -O2; covariant-refused.cpp and memptr-refused.cpp keep the refusals left (test 17); sample-mangle `useCovariantVb` and `vftableNames` (test 15: the thunks, the extended table and the vftable names as cl's; and a check of its own for the vftables, which cppparse keeps static: all 33 cl defines are named alike) |
| B13 | (found by A1's own code) a nested type declared after the inline member body that uses it: `struct Outer { int make() { Item it; ... } struct Item {...}; };` is a parse error ("expected ';' after the expression"), since the parser reads the body in place and knows the class's member function names only (3.3.6: a member body sees the whole class) | fixed (2026-10-21): the class body's pre-scan (CxxParser classSpecifier) also declares the names of the nested classes, enums and typedefs (the identifier before a typedef's `;` or `[`, or the `(*Fn)` of a function pointer). front.cpp `nestedtype::` |
| B12 | a name found in a derived class on one path and in its base on another (`struct B : A { int a; }; struct C : A {}; struct D : B, C {}; d.a`, and `a` inside D's members): accepted (cppparse takes B::a), cl C2385 | fixed (2026-10-23): member lookup collects, per base subobject, the first class on its path that declares the name (the path is the complete object or a virtual base, then the non-virtual bases), drops a declaration whose subobject is a base subobject of another one found (10.2/6, dominance: below it on its path or inside one of its class's virtual bases), and the name is ambiguous, S144 as cl's C2385, when what is left comes from subobjects of different classes, or from several subobjects of one class with a non-static member among the declarations (10.2/2; Sema::memberLookupAmbiguous). Checked for `d.a` / `p->f()` (exprMember, which took the "unrelated bases" test before) and for a data member or member function named without an object: unqualified inside a member function (implicit `this->a`, the innermost enclosing class that finds it), qualified (`D::a`, `D::s`) and in `&D::a` (Sema::ambiguousMemberName, from exprName and exprCall); a static member, an enumerator or a type of a base held twice stays unambiguous, as does a declaration hidden through a virtual base, unless that base is also held non-virtually (`struct Y : L, W` with `L : virtual V`, `W : V`: cl C2385 too). ambiguous.cpp (test 17: the 17 errors cl gives, on cl's lines, the count checked) has B::a vs A::a with and without an object, unqualified in members, `YD::a`, `&YD::a`, a static member, two A subobjects inside a member and the mixed virtual/non-virtual base; front.cpp `ambig::` runs the valid forms (static members, enumerators and types through two paths, dominance through a virtual base with an object, unqualified, qualified and as a pointer to member) against cl at -O0 and -O2 |
| B14 | (found by C3's tests) `&&` and `||` with an operand of class type always took the built-in operators, the class operands converted to bool: valarray's `operator&&` and `operator||` were never called (S109 "no conversion from valarray<double> to bool"), nor any user-declared one (13.5) | fixed (2026-10-23): with a class operand, Sema::exprBinary first looks for a user-declared operator (Sema::classOperator: a member of the left operand's class, then the free functions of ordinary and argument-dependent lookup, templates deduced) and makes the expression a call of it, both operands evaluated and nothing short-circuited, as for any operator function (13.5: an overloaded operator is a function call); without one the operands are converted to bool and the built-in operator short-circuits as before (IrGen::condition and value take a Binary with a semaSym for the call). A stream with `<valarray>` in view (`in >> n && n > 3`) still takes the built-in operator: no valarray template deduces from a stream. front.cpp `logicop::` (a free operator&& returning int, a member operator|| and operator&& of an explicit class, chained, ADL in another namespace, both operands evaluated and counted, the built-in short circuit of a class with operator bool logged) against cl at -O0 and -O2; sample-lib valarray.cpp `a && b`, `a || (b - 2.0)`, `a && 0.0`, `0.0 || (a - 1.0)` (test 15) |
| B15 | (found by C3's tests) a base class named through a typedef (`class B : public std::streambuf`, `struct D1 : IntBase` with `typedef Base<int> IntBase;`): the parser did not know the base's member types in the class (`int_type`, `traits_type`, `value_type`: "unknown type name"), nor through the typedef as a qualifier (`std::streambuf::int_type`) | fixed (2026-10-23): a typedef that names a class (a class or a template-id, at namespace or class scope) is recorded as an alias of the class's named scope (CxxParser registerDeclaredNames, m_aliases), and findNamedScope takes an alias where the class's own name would be, at each enclosing scope as for the names themselves; so a base spelled with the typedef gives the class the base's type and template names, and a qualifier spelled with it finds them (`IntBase::value_type`, `std::streambuf::traits_type::eof()`). Sema resolved such bases already. front.cpp `tdbase::` (a class template's instantiation and a plain class through typedefs, one of another namespace and one through two typedefs, nested types, enumerators) against cl at -O0 and -O2; sample-lib streambuf.cpp derives from `std::streambuf` and `std::wstreambuf` (test 15) |

(c) Library:

| # | Case | State |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | `string::at` (and `substr`, `erase`, `insert`, `replace`, `compare` with a position, the `(str, pos)` constructor) abort instead of throwing `std::out_of_range`; `string::copy` is missing | fixed (2026-10-21): they throw `out_of_range("invalid string position")` through `__throw_out_of_range`, declared in `<string>` before the class and defined inline at the end of `<stdexcept>` (which `<string>` includes last; either order of inclusion works), and `copy(char*, n, pos)` exists. sample-lib stringThrows (test 15, lib.ps1) |
| C2 | `map::value_type` is `pair<K, V>`: `M::value_type("a", 1)`, `m.insert(std::pair<const K, V>(...))` and `std::pair<const K, V>& r = *it` are refused | fixed (2026-10-21): the tree holds `pair<const K, V>` (copy-constructed nodes, never assigned), pair has the converting constructor template of 20.2.2 for `insert(make_pair(k, v))`. The compiler with it: a converting constructor template of the target class takes part in an implicit conversion (Sema::convert instantiates it from the argument, 13.3.1.4), and `M::value_type(k, v)` with a typedef M, which the parser cannot tell from a call, is the functional cast it is (exprCall). sample-lib mapValueType (test 15, lib.ps1) |
| C3 | `std::string` is not `basic_string` (no `wstring`, no wide streams); `<complex>`, `<valarray>`, `<strstream>`, `<locale>`, `<streambuf>` are not provided | fixed (2026-10-23), in three commits. (1) `<string>` is `basic_string<charT, traits, Allocator>` (21.3) with `typedef basic_string<char> string` and `typedef basic_string<wchar_t> wstring`, over a zero-terminated buffer from the allocator, pointer iterators: every member of 21.3 (the iterator-range forms with the integer dispatch of 21.3/6), the non-member operators, swap, npos defined out of class; C1's throwing members and `copy`, and the old class's `compare(s, n)`, are kept; appending, inserting, replacing or assigning from the string itself is safe now (the old class read freed memory when it grew: `s += s`). `char_traits<char>` and `char_traits<wchar_t>` (21.1; wchar_t 16 bits, eof WEOF) with `fpos`, `streampos`, `streamoff`, `streamsize` are in `<__char_traits>`, `allocator` in `<__allocator>`; the names are cl's (`?$basic_string@DU?$char_traits@D@std@@V?$allocator@D@2@@std@@`). (2) The streams are the templates of clause 27 over a basic_streambuf, with the char and wchar_t typedefs: `<streambuf>` (basic_streambuf, its get and put areas, the virtual functions of 27.5.2.4), `<__ios_base>` (ios_base with MSVC's flag, state and openmode values, xalloc, iword, pword, register_callback, imbue, getloc), `<ios>` (basic_ios: the state, exceptions() throwing ios_base::failure, tie, rdbuf, copyfmt, fill, imbue, widen, narrow; the ios_base manipulators), `<ostream>` and `<istream>` (basic_ostream, basic_istream, basic_iostream with virtual bases and their sentries; tellp, seekp, tellg, seekg, get, getline, read, readsome, putback, unget, ignore, peek; endl, ends, flush and ws as function templates), `<sstream>` (basic_stringbuf, istringstream, ostringstream, stringstream and the wide ones), `<fstream>` (basic_filebuf over the C runtime's FILE, unbuffered so that C and C++ output to one FILE stay in order; ifstream, ofstream, fstream and the wide ones), `<iostream>` (cin, cout, cerr, clog, wcin, wcout, wcerr, wclog, defined in libcpp/iostream.cpp with cl's names, `?cout@std@@3V?$basic_ostream@DU?$char_traits@D@std@@@1@A`), `<iomanip>` for both character types, and `<iterator>`'s stream iterators over charT with istreambuf_iterator and ostreambuf_iterator. `<iosfwd>` includes the stream headers. (3) `<complex>`: complex<T> with its operators, the stream operators, real, imag, abs, arg, norm, conj, polar, exp, log, log10, sqrt, sin, cos, tan, sinh, cosh, tanh, the four pow; `<valarray>`: valarray with every operator, computed assignment and member of 26.3.2, slice, gslice, slice_array, gslice_array, mask_array, indirect_array, the transcendentals; `<strstream>` (annex D.7): strstreambuf (dynamic, with the program's allocation functions, or over the program's array), istrstream, ostrstream, strstream; `<locale>`: locale with its facets and ids, use_facet, has_facet, the classic locale's ctype<char> (over a mask table), ctype<wchar_t>, numpunct and collate (in `<__locale>`, which every stream includes), the convenience functions of 22.1.3, ctype_byname, numpunct_byname, collate_byname; a numpunct the program installs points and groups the numbers a stream writes and reads; `<cmath>` declares asin, acos, sinh, cosh, tanh too. (4, 2026-10-23) Thread safety, found by the health gate on abb30cc: the self-built cppparse's multi-threaded `--health .` died now and then with 0xC0000374 (heap corruption) and wrote nothing (`-j 1` and the cl-built cppparse were fine). Every stream holds locales, and the global imp is shared by the streams of all threads; its reference count was a plain `size_t`, so concurrent copies and destructions lost counts and freed the imp while in use. The imps' reference counts and the facets' holder counts now change by the runtime's interlocked operations (`cppparse_interlocked_increment`, `_decrement` and the new `_compare_exchange` of runtime/cppparse_rt.cpp, declared in `<__locale>`, so that cl's builds of the headers (mix.ps1) and cppparse's use the same); the classic imp, `locale::classic()`, the global imp and ctype<char>'s classic table are made once in static locals (whose initialisation is thread-safe in cl's code and in cppparse's, cl's protocol); a facet id draws its index by an interlocked increment and stores it by compare-exchange (two threads asking first agree; a drawn number may go unused); `locale::global` keeps the old imp referenced for good (a thread may have read the old pointer and not yet counted it) and does not free it; `ios_base::xalloc` counts interlocked. Nothing else in the library is shared between streams (the facets of the classic locale are read only; the standard stream objects are made before `main`). sample-lib threads.cpp runs eight threads of string streams, strings and locales started together before any stream exists, against cl's build (the old headers fail it 19 runs in 20 with heap corruption); the self-built cppparse ran `--health .` multi-threaded 30 times in a row with no crash and the same output each time. (5, 2026-10-23) Speed: C3's headers made the self-built cppparse (an -O0 build, where every call is made) slower than before C3 (--health . 5 to 9 s against 4 to 5; selfhost.ps1's stage 2 about 200 s against 115 to 135). A sampling profile of the self-built cppparse (stack samples through dbghelp, -j 1) showed the heap (malloc and free of every string, 30 to 37 % of the time already before C3) and the extra layers C3 put between a string or stream member and its work (traits, allocator, sentry, a virtual xsputn per insertion, a get area re-made after every write, a file read three fgetc and two ungetc per character). The headers now: basic_string keeps up to 15 chars (7 wchar_t) in the object, MSVC's layout (the characters or the buffer's address, the size, the capacity; the capacity tells which), with no pointer into itself, so vector still moves strings bytewise; copies and moves of characters are memcpy and memmove (what 21.1.1 defines traits::copy and move to be), and for char_traits<char> (`__is_char_traits`) the members call memcmp, strlen and memchr themselves; append, push_back, +=, assign, compare, find, c_str, size, the copies and the comparisons do their work in place; char_traits<wchar_t> uses wcslen and wmemset. basic_streambuf's six area pointers are public under reserved names, so that the streams read and write the areas themselves: an inserter of a good stream with nothing tied, no width and no unitbuf needs no sentry and copies into the put area when it fits; basic_stringbuf's put area is its string's whole storage (a write that fits is a copy, the high-water mark of the put pointer is the end of the characters) and its get area its characters; basic_filebuf<char> over a file it opened has a 4 KB buffer of its own (a put area when the file is opened for output only, a get area when it is opened for input only in binary mode; text input, a file opened for both, a wide buffer and the standard streams stay unbuffered: the C runtime's line ends would make a text get area's position unknown, and C and C++ output to one FILE stay in order), writes char straight with fwrite and reads long runs with fread; `os << sb` moves sb's get area a run at a time; istream's sentry, `>> string` and getline scan the get area (getline with memchr) and ctype<char>'s table; a stream made with the classic locale takes its ctype from a cache and its punctuation as known; ios_base's destructor makes no call for a stream without callbacks or words; the integer formatting writes its digits backwards into the caller's buffer. Found on the way and fixed: `getline` and `>> string` erased the string when the sentry failed, where 21.3.7.9 (and cl) leave it. sample-lib buffers.cpp (test 15) checks the edges against cl at -O0 and -O2: strings at 15/16 chars, appended, inserted, replaced and assigned from themselves, swapped between the object and the heap, moved by vector; a string stream's put area as it grows, after seekp, in|out, app and ate; a file longer than the buffers written and read back by getline, >>, read, tellg and seekg (across the buffer's end), `<< rdbuf()`, and a text-mode read. Measured on this machine (two runs each, while another agent worked on it; pre-C3 is 457b29d, C3 abb30cc): selfhost.ps1's stage 2 150 and 110 s before C3, 212 and 209 s with C3, 117 and 116 s now; `--health .` of the self-built cppparse (median of three) 4.7 and 4.5 s multi-threaded and 7.1 and 6.5 s with `-j 1` before C3, 6.0 and 6.7 s and 8.6 and 10.0 s with C3, 3.2 and 3.2 s and 4.4 and 4.5 s now (the cl-built cppparse, on MSVC's library, 1.7 to 2.3 s throughout); a string and stream micro-benchmark built by cppparse at -O0 (12 operations of 200000 rounds: short strings made, += of chars, a + b + literal, comparisons, finds, vector<string>, ostringstream made and written, ifstream into a string with << rdbuf(), ofstream, getline; median of five) 686 ms before C3, 1177 ms with C3, 558 ms now. Stage 2 is now as fast as before C3 though it compiles more: the listings of the 39 self-built units are 229 MB (39 MB of objects) with C3's headers against 145 MB (27 MB) before, 234 MB (40 MB) now; the self-built compiler itself is a third faster than before C3 (`--health`), and the self-built cppparse ran `--health .` multi-threaded 30 times with no crash and the same output. (6, 2026-10-23) complex<float>, complex<double> and complex<long double> are the explicit specialisations of 26.2.3: conversions widen implicitly (float to double, float and double to long double) and narrow only explicitly; the values and transcendentals are computed in the type's own precision (`__complex_math<T>`: the C runtime's float functions sqrtf, expf, logf, sinf, cosf, sinhf, coshf, atan2f for float, which `<cmath>` now declares; long double is double on this target), the arithmetic shared through `__complex_ops<T>`. Header workarounds for a compiler gap, recorded in `<complex>`: inside an explicit specialisation cppparse takes the class's own name, injected or with its argument, for the primary template's instantiation (as naming ctype<char> in its own base clause did), so the specialisations name themselves nowhere in their bodies: the narrowing constructors are templates (a constructor defined after the class does not match its declaration either, S116), assignment is the implicit copy (a value of the type converts through the constructor, as 26.2.3's operator=(float) would assign), and the compound assignments are functions outside the class, through real() and imag(). sample-lib complexf.cpp (test 15) against cl at -O0 and -O2: the sizes, the conversions both ways, an overload set that is ambiguous unless narrowing is explicit, float arithmetic, every value and transcendental of complex<float> and some of complex<long double>, the stream operators of each type. (7, 2026-10-23) num_put and num_get (22.2.2), in `<__num_facets>` with istreambuf_iterator and ostreambuf_iterator (moved there from `<iterator>`): num_put<charT, OutputIterator> with put and do_put for bool, long, unsigned long, long long, unsigned long long, double, long double and const void* (stage 1 the text printf makes with the conversion the flags select, stage 2 widened by the locale's ctype with its numpunct's point and thousands separators, stage 3 padded to the width with the fill: after a left-adjusted number, after its sign or 0x when internal, before it otherwise; the width reset), num_get<charT, InputIterator> with get and do_get for bool, long, unsigned short, unsigned int, unsigned long, long long, unsigned long long, float, double, long double and void* (stage 2 the characters the grammar allows: a sign, a 0x prefix, the digits of the base basefield selects (none: from the prefix, as %i), the thousands separators, whose grouping is checked; the point and an exponent; stage 3: failbit when no digit was taken or the value does not fit, which stores the type's nearest limit, as C++11 and cl do; a minus sign negates an unsigned value; eofbit when the input ended). The streams write and read numbers through them (27.6.2.5.2, 27.6.1.2.2: short and int through long, with a range check for short); a locale holds the library's facets when it has none of its own (`__default_facet`, which use_facet, has_facet and combine consult), so a num_put or num_get the program installs is the one called; the library's own num_put the stream does in place (the same formatting code, straight into the buffer: no iterator, no virtual call), so the speed of (5) stays. The formatting follows printf where C3's did not: no '+' for an unsigned value under showpos, no 0x for zero under showbase, internal padding. sample-lib numfacets.cpp (test 15) against cl at -O0 and -O2: printf's rules, the paddings, negative values in hexadecimal, a num_put of the program's (bracketing longs) through a stream and directly through ostreambuf_iterator, has_facet, grouping written and read (and a bad grouping), num_get's bases, ranges, signs, floating values, bool with and without boolalpha, a num_get of the program's (reading longs in hexadecimal) and num_get called directly, and the wide streams. (8, 2026-10-23) 64-bit file positions: streamoff and fpos held 64-bit offsets already, but basic_filebuf sought with fseek and told with ftell, whose offset is a 32-bit long here; it now uses the C runtime's _fseeki64 and _ftelli64 (declared in `<cstdio>`), so tellg, seekg, tellp and seekp of ifstream, ofstream and fstream (buffered or not) work beyond 2 GB, open with ate as well. sample-lib bigfile.cpp (test 15) against cl at -O0 and -O2: fpos arithmetic past 3 GB, then a file made sparse (FSCTL_SET_SPARSE, so it takes no space) written at 3 GB + 5 through fstream, read back there, sought from the end and by beg + cur, appended to through a buffered ofstream and read through a buffered ifstream. (9, 2026-10-23) Speed, second step: a profile of an ostringstream benchmark found two division instructions on every insertion in -O0 code: a char pointer difference (the put area's room, `pend - pnext`) is compiled as a signed division by the element size, 1, and the decimal digits of a number were taken by 64-bit division by ten. The put area's room is now compared in bytes (the addresses as integers against n * sizeof(charT): `<ostream>`, and the stringbuf's and filebuf's xsputn), the stringbuf's positions and basic_string's find results are counted from byte addresses, and a number of 32 bits is divided by ten as (v * 0xCCCCCCCD) >> 35, its length returned beside it. Third, the self-build's listings had grown from 234 to 276 MB with (7): cppparse analyses every member function of a stream class it instantiates (and every function template that overload resolution looks at), and emits the virtual functions of every polymorphic class named in them, so each unit that made a stream compiled num_put's and num_get's virtual functions for char and wchar_t. The streams now reach a program's num_put and the locale's num_get through `__num_out` and `__num_in`, explicitly specialised for char and wchar_t with char_traits to call plain functions `__num_put_out` and `__num_get_in` that libcpp/iostream.cpp defines (`__num_put_call` and `__num_get_call` of `<__num_facets>`, which other streams reach through the primary templates), and a stream finds its locale's num_put by an id in a plain base (`__num_put_id`, as ctype<char> takes its id from `__facet_id`), so the facets are compiled once, in libcpp. Measured after it (pre-C3 run between the two): selfhost.ps1's stage 2 108 and 110 s (pre-C3 102 s in the same session; 110 to 150 s in earlier ones), the listings 237 MB (40 MB of objects); `--health .` of the self-built cppparse 3.0 and 3.1 s multi-threaded and 4.3 s with `-j 1` (pre-C3 3.9 and 5.7 s); the micro-benchmark 390 ms (pre-C3 514 ms); 30 multi-threaded `--health .` runs with no crash and the same output. Stage 2 is at par with before C3 while compiling 63 % more code (the C3 headers' templates, and the wide stream classes, whose virtual functions cppparse compiles in every unit that names them in a typedef; next work: the classic locale's facets made in libcpp rather than in every unit, and the wide streams' virtual functions likewise). Left out: codecvt, the monetary, time and messages facets, named locales other than "C" and "" (bad locale name), locale::global does not call setlocale; basic_string keeps no allocator object (an allocator's state is not kept: holding one would cost every string's construction a call at -O0) and has pointer iterators; a wide file or wcout writes each character as one byte and fails on one above 255 (as MSVC's "C" locale; no codecvt); `sync_with_stdio` is a no-op (the standard streams always write through the C runtime). The compiler, two changes: partial ordering gives its unique types a member scope (Sema::atLeastAsSpecialized: `std::swap(s, t)` with `<utility>`'s swap in view crashed the compiler, as `swap(v, w)` of two vectors did before), and the name of a function template or of an overloaded function given to a pointer or reference to function takes the specialisation or overload of the target's type (13.4, 14.8.2.2; Sema::convert, converted, exprBinary, resolveOverload, Conversion::function in Sema.hpp: `cout << endl` with endl a template instantiated nothing and referenced `std__endl`, LNK2019; of overloaded functions the first declared was taken). Header workarounds for compiler gaps, each recorded at its place in the headers: the character and string inserters and extractors are members of basic_ostream and basic_istream (a viable member operator hides the non-member ones in cppparse, against 13.3.1.2: `cout << 'c'` would print 99); getline, the `<iomanip>` operators and complex's stream operators are templates and plain functions for the char and wchar_t streams too (no template argument is deduced from a derived class, 14.8.2.1/3); the sentries have operator! (`!obj` through operator bool is refused); `<iosfwd>` includes the stream headers and the template defaults are on the definitions (a forward declaration's defaults are not taken over, and a specialisation named before its template's definition stays incomplete); iterator_traits<const T*> comes before <T*> (the first matching partial specialisation is taken, not the most specialised: reverse_iterator<const char*> did not compile); basic_string's `insert(p, c = charT())` and istream's `ignore(n = 1, delim = eof())` are overloads (a class template member's default argument is analysed for partial ordering's unique types, with errors); substr builds a local (a functional cast with the injected class name and several arguments is taken for a call); basic_ios sets its state without `sb ? goodbit : badbit` (an lvalue of a static const member, which has no definition); ctype<char> takes its id from a base __facet_id<0> (naming ctype<char> in its own base clause instantiated the primary template too); locale has plain operator() members for string and wstring beside the template (row A7). Found, not changed: the member types of a base named by a typedef (`class B : public std::streambuf`) are not types in the class for the parser (`int_type`: syntax error; the tests spell the base `std::basic_streambuf<char>`; fixed since, row B15); `&&` and `||` of class operands are the built-in ones (valarray's operator&& and operator|| are not called: S109; fixed since, row B14). Test 15: sample-lib strings.cpp, wstreams.cpp, streambuf.cpp, complex.cpp, valarray.cpp, strstream.cpp, locale.cpp and threads.cpp built by cl with its own headers and by cppparse at -O0 and -O2, the same output; sample-mangle libnames.cpp, the 23 names a unit defines over the strings, the stream classes, complex, valarray, locale and the strstream classes, and the names of the 8 standard streams, are cl's (its own headers). sample-front sizeof-incomplete.cpp declares its own template (`<iosfwd>` defines the streams now) |

(d) Won't fix, with the reason (re-checked 2026-10-21):

- The order of copies and destructors when a by-value handler rethrows into another by-value handler: cppparse unwinds
  first and copies the outer handler's parameter on entry to the handler, as 15.2 and 15.3 describe; cl copies it before it
  unwinds (its two-phase handling). Same objects, cl's own order; stays.
- `(double)~(int)3e9`, `(int)3e9 * x` and `const int ci = 3e9` as a constant expression: a floating value outside the
  integer's range is undefined behaviour (4.9/1); cl's untruncated 64-bit value is its own, not asked for.
- NRVO for a local in a nested block or for different locals on disjoint paths at -O2 (cl /O2 elides, cppparse copies):
  copy elision is permitted, never required (12.8/15); -O0 matches cl /Od.
- The class temporaries of all arguments built before a call inside another argument (`Holder h(mkV(35), takeV(V(36)))`:
  cl V(36), takeV, ~V(36), V(35)): unspecified (5.2.2/8), cl's own scheme.
- A value template recursing 5000 deep stops at the 400-deep expression limit (S111/S114) rather than S148: cl rejects it too.
- `catch (std::exception e)` sliced from a `runtime_error` loses the message: C++98's `std::exception` carries none (18.6.1);
  cl keeps it through an MSVC extension.
- `typeid` without `<typeinfo>` but with a user-declared `type_info`: accepted (cl C2027); ill-formed code, no wrong code.
- M12's C4701 (potentially uninitialised), C4458/C4459 (a local hiding a member or a global) and cl's note lines: not planned.
- M7's exceptions across cl-compiled frames (cl's `__CxxFrameHandler3` model): done as `--eh=cl` (not the default), docs/design-cl-exceptions.md.
- M10 (CodeView types, variables and source checksums) is closed with stage 5 (2026-11-26). What the design leaves to rows of their own: inlinee records (after GRIDE3's L3), value tracking for scalars assigned more than once, the source lines of types; and two sessions by hand in Visual Studio's debugger (docs/design-codeview.md, status).
- Code size against cl /O2: the plan of the last section, next work.

## Plan

Three lanes, worked in parallel on their own branches; each item gets a regression test, and each fix runs
the full suite plus matrix, lib, life, selfhost, mix, cprog and mapcompare before its commit.
