A data member pointer is an int offset with null -1 (`int P::* pm = 0;` is FFFFFFFFh), with virtual bases {offset, vbtable offset of the base's entry} (null {0, -1}; the offset of a member outside a virtual base counts from the vbptr's subobject, which entry 0 leads to: cl's `obj + vbptr offset + vbtable[entry] + offset`), in the unspecified model (sizeof taken while the class was incomplete, row S10) {offset, vbptr offset, vbtable offset} (null {0, 0, -1}; cl reads the object itself unless the entry is non-zero). A member function pointer is a code pointer (a vcall thunk `??_9P@@$BA@AA` (slot 0), `$B7AA` (slot 1), `mov rax,[rcx]; jmp [rax+off]` COMDAT, for a virtual one; `&AB::fb` of an override first held by a secondary vftable is AB's thunk with cl's adjustment 16), with multiple inheritance {code pointer, int this adjustment} (null {0, 0}), with virtual bases {code pointer, adjustment, vbtable offset}, unspecified {code pointer, adjustment, vbptr offset, vbtable offset} (null's last field -1). Conversions: base -> derived adds the base's offset (data: unless null; functions: always, as cl), into a class with virtual bases sets the entry (through a virtual base: the offset in it), between two classes with virtual bases copies the value when the derived class shares the base's vbptr (cl does the same; one that does not share it remaps the entry through cl's displacement map since 2026-10-23, B11), derived -> base (static_cast) subtracts (data: unless null) or drops the adjustment (functions, as cl's `castQMf`). `==`/`!=` convert both to the more derived class's type and compare every field, a function's beyond the code pointer only when it is not null; `pm` as a condition is "not null". ABI as cl: 4 and 8 bytes in a register (also the {offset, entry} pair), 12/16/24 bytes by the address of a copy and through a hidden result pointer that a member function takes BEFORE this (`?rq@Ret@@QEAAP8QQ@@EAAHXZP8Q@@EAAHXZ@Z`: rcx the result, rdx this; checked with cl /FAs), unlike class results. Static storage: `&C::m` and null are static data (cl emits dynamic initialisers for the converted ones; the bytes are the same); zero-initialised objects get cl's null values exactly where cl writes them: a pointer to member, an array of them at any depth, and the pointer-to-member members of a class without bases (not arrays of them, not nested classes, not arrays of classes: `Outer go[2]`, `Two gtwo`, `Der gder` stay zero in cl); value-initialisation zeroes (cl too); a brace list's omitted elements are null for pointers to members and arrays of them, zero for omitted classes (`Outer o = {}` zero, `Outer o = {{}}` -1, `Holder h[2] = {{..}}` h[1] zero). Mangling: `PEQP@@H`, `PERP@@H` (const member), `P8P@@EAAHXZ`, `P8P@@EBAHXZ` (const function), `Q8` for a const pointer, variables `?pm@@3PEQP@@HEQ1@`, `?arr@@3PAPEQP@@HA`. Calls `(obj.*pmf)(x)`, `(p->*pmf)(x)` are indirect calls with the adjusted this (sema typed them S109 before). Test 17 front.cpp `memptr::` (-O0, -O2 against cl, every word of every representation), test 13 sample-layout MP/MQ/MH/VL (both compilers make, pass, apply, compare and store them, -O0..-O3, cl's derived class through cppparse's thunk); `sizeof(int I1::*)` with I1 incomplete is unchanged (front.cpp `mplater::`). Still refused (S132, sample-front/memptr-refused.cpp): a conversion into a class with virtual bases that does not share the base's vbptr (`VW : V, W` from `int W::*`: the vbtable entry would need remapping), down from a class with virtual bases to a base, and between two classes whose models are both unspecified; a member pointer as a template argument stays a sema error (`TA<&P::b>`: cl mangles it `$0M@`, `$1?h@P@@QEAAHXZ`) | done | medium | layout |
| 19 | covariant return through a secondary base (`CD* CD::self()` over `SB* SB::self()`, SB at 16): done (2026-10-14) as cl lays it out, checked with cl /FAs and dumpbin on every shape (Sema.cpp completeClass, returnAdjustment; IrGen.cpp registerMetadata; X86.cpp addReturnThunk; MangleSema.cpp msvcMangleReturnThunk). When some overridden slot's return class holds the overrider's return class... (precisely: the overrider's return class holds the overridden function's return class at a non-zero offset), cl gives the overrider a slot of its own at the end of the vftable that holds the overridden function (in the primary table among the class's new virtual functions in declaration order: `L : K { ln; CD* k(); lm }` is `k(thunk), kk, ln, k, lm`), and every slot it overrides holds a return-adjusting thunk, named as the function, non-virtual, returning the overridden type (`?self@CD@@QEAAPEAUSB@@XZ`, `?six@CD@@QEAAPEAUSB@@HHHHNH@Z`): a frame, the stack arguments staged again, the call, then a non-null pointer (a reference always) moved by the offset; further overriders (`E : CD { E* self(); }`) get thunks in every slot, also where the move is 0 (`?self@E@@QEAAPEAUCD@@XZ`), and a new slot again. An override whose moves are all 0 (`PD : SB { PD* self(); }`) stays in place, as cl. Test 17 front.cpp `covariant::` (-O0, -O2 against cl: through every base, references, six parameters, an override chain, a primary-table case, null results); test 13 sample-layout RA/RB/RC: cppparse's thunks called by cl through RB*, RC* and RB&, cl's derived class RD with covariant overrides called by cppparse, -O0..-O3; a throw through the thunk unwinds in both EH modes (scratch cov3). Refused with S131 (sample-front/covariant-refused.cpp): a return conversion through a virtual base (cl's thunk reads the vbtable), a slot that needs a this adjustment as well as the return adjustment (cl's `$R` thunks; both done 2026-10-23, B11: an adjustor to the return thunk), and a variadic covariant function | done | medium | layout |

## Deferred

- 15 (MSVC ABI parity), only needed for mixed links with cl objects: classes of 1/2/4/8 bytes passed and
  returned in registers is done (IrGen::regParamType/regReturnType, sample-abi in Test 13); static locals
  guarded with cl's thread-safe-statics protocol and names is done (sample-statics in Test 13, with
  _Init_thread_abort for a throwing initializer); class results through pointers to functions,
  `(*fp)(x)`, variadic class arguments and cl's own-members rule for results in rax are done (sample-abi);
  a static local cl initialises as a constant (`static int u = int();`, `static double d = 1.0/4;`,
  `static float f = 1.5f*2;`, value-initialisation, casts) is static data with cl's folded bytes and no
  guard (IrGen::foldArith/convertFolded; the bits go out as MASM's encoded reals), so the `$TSS<n>` after
  it match cl's (konstK in sample-statics, cl inlining it); statics in nested blocks get cl's block
  numbers (IrGen::numberStaticBlocks: the body 2, then in source order each compound statement, if and
  each of its branches, for/while/do/switch; checked against cl for blocks, branches, else-if chains,
  loops, switch, try/catch, labels and conditions with declarations; nestedN in sample-statics); open:
  statics inside the member functions of a local class are not checked against cl; exceptions do not cross between cl's
  and cppparse's runtimes (a cl throw into a cppparse frame fails fast); (vtable slots grouped by overload and the
  destructor slot holding the deleting destructor are done).
- design-table-exceptions.md section 7: an exception edge from every call to its landing pad, then GVN and
  register values across handlers; the main lever on code size (1.55 times cl's over all common functions of the matrix sample at d9309fa). The
  register allocator has these edges now (computeEhEdges); the IR and the optimiser do not (a call inside a region
  does not end its block), so pinHandlerLocals, the GVN guard and handlersSelfContained stay.

## Other known limits (found by the reviews of 2026-10-13)

- (found 2026-10-20 by the M11 checks of the review's misc1.cpp and misc3.cpp, fixed 2026-10-21) a user-defined
  `operator,` was bypassed: `Cm operator,(const Cm& a, const Cm& b)` with `Cm c = (Cm(1), Cm(2));` gave the
  built-in comma's result (2; cl 102), a silent miscompile. Sema's Comma case now looks the operator up when an
  operand is a class (13.3.1.2: a member of the left operand's class, else ordinary and argument-dependent lookup,
  with the usual conversions: `(k, Cm(7))` with an int k calls it through `Cm(int)`, as cl does), and IrGen calls
  it (value, lvalue, operatorCall); the built-in comma stays for the rest. And the address of an `extern "C"`
  function used as a value (`CFn fn = cfunc;`, the gap analysis's `&std::printf`) was referenced as the variable
  symbol `g_cfunc` (LNK2019): X86Module::globalSymbol took an IR name without a parameter list for a variable, and
  an extern "C" function's IR name is its plain name; IrGen::registerMetadata now registers those names
  (registerCFunction). front.cpp `commaop::` and `cfunc::` against cl; the review's misc1.cpp prints what cl
  prints in full.

- (fixed 2026-10-14) Test 2 (the old pipeline reading the whole `cpp` folder) overflowed its stack when a class named
  `T` was anywhere in the folder: the old instantiator (analyzer/Instantiate.cpp argumentsDependent) took a template
  argument for concrete when its names matched classes of the folder, so `Matrix<T, R - 1, C - 1>` in Matrix.hpp's
  submatrix was instantiated with `T` the class and `C - 1` (front.cpp has a `struct C`), each instantiation asking for
  a deeper one until the IR builder recursed over `C - 1 - 1 - ...`. A non-type argument with a name and an operator
  that does not fold is now dependent, and argument texts over 512 characters are not instantiated; front.cpp has a
  `tclass::T` so test 2 keeps checking it. Debug builds of cppparse print the stack on an overflow (driver/main.cpp).
- cl builds a handler's by-value parameter before it unwinds the frames and handlers the exception leaves (MSVC's
  two-phase handling); cppparse unwinds first, so the order of copies and destructors differs when a handler is
  left by a throw that an outer by-value handler catches. Repro: `struct B { int v; B(int x) : v(x) {} B(const B&
  o) : v(o.v + 10) { printf("cB%d ", v); } ~B() { printf("~B%d ", v); } };` then `try { try { throw B(10); } catch
  (B b) { throw b; } } catch (B b2) { printf("got%d ", b2.v); }`: cl `cB20 cB30 cB40 ~B20 ~B10 got40 ~B40 ~B30`,
  cppparse `cB20 cB30 ~B20 ~B10 cB40 got40 ~B40 ~B30` (the same objects, cB40 later).
- (fixed, row 18) Calls through pointers to members (`(obj.*pmf)(x)`, `(p->*pmf)(x)`) were rejected by sema and every
  other use of pointers to members refused by --compile6; they compile to cl's representations now.
- (fixed, S12) A float literal with an `f` suffix was converted decimal -> double -> float, which differs from cl's
  direct decimal -> float rounding when the double is exactly halfway between two floats; such a static took the
  guarded dynamic path. common/Literal.cpp floatingLiteralValue now rounds once (exact comparison of the decimal
  with the midpoint); `static float h = 1.00000005960464477550f;` is static data 0x3F800001 with no guard.
- (fixed, S8/S9) Out-of-range float -> integer conversions of constants are folded as cl folds them (modulo 2^N,
  the untruncated value kept for a floating conversion that follows), in code and in static data. Left: cl applies
  `~` to the untruncated 64-bit value (`(double)~(int)3e9` is -3000000001 in cl, cppparse -(B2D05E00h)-1 =
  1294967295), and a folded constant converted to a floating type after another operand was evaluated
  (`(int)3e9 * x` with a double variable x: cl 3e9 * x, cppparse -1294967296 * x); a const integer variable
  initialised by a floating constant (`const int ci = 3e9;`) is not an integral constant expression in sema. Checked 2026-11-05: that is C++98's rule (5.19/1 admits a floating literal in an integral constant expression only when cast; `const int ci = 3.0; int a[ci];` is S114, cl accepts it as an extension), so not a gap (row P18 below).
- (fixed) At -O2 a run-time double -> unsigned conversion of 3000000000.0 gave 2147483648: the optimiser folded
  it through a 32-bit `long`. Floating <-> integer conversions now follow cl's code at every level and in the
  folder (X86.cpp Conv, Optimizer.cpp floatToInt; sample-front `conv::`).
- (fixed) 64-bit integer literals: `4294967295ULL` was sign-extended to 2^64-1 and wider literals kept their low
  32 bits. Sema reads all 64 bits (integerLiteralValue) and types them as cl (int, unsigned int for hexadecimal
  and octal, long, unsigned long, long long, unsigned long long); IrGen builds a value outside the 32-bit
  sign-extended immediates with bitConstant; a case label converts from its own type (`case 4294967295U:` of an
  unsigned long long switch compared with 2^64-1). Left, refused or merely different from cl: Expr::semaValue
  is still a 32-bit `long`, so such a literal is no constant expression: `case 4294967296ULL:`, an array bound
  or a template argument of that value is an error (S114 and the like, not wrong code); a global or static
  initialised with it gets a dynamic initialiser (a static local a guard) where cl emits static data
  (`unsigned long long b = 4294967295ULL;`: cl `DQ 0FFFFFFFFh`, cppparse `DQ 0` plus an initialiser; the value
  is right once main runs). Fix: a 64-bit semaValue (SemaExpr folding, constantValue, IrGen constant tables).
- (review of the lane C commits, fixed) the C2584/C4584 check took a non-virtual base of a virtual base for a
  virtual one (cl looks only at the edge to the base: `W : V; XV : virtual W; T7 : V, XV` warns, `T8 : virtual
  V, XV` is an error); the member-pointer field refusal fired on implicit instantiations cl accepts
  (`sizeof(Holder<P>*)`; instantiations are no longer refused, using their member still is); member-pointer
  sizes for an incomplete class (cl's unspecified model: data 12, functions 24) and for a vfptr added in front
  of a non-polymorphic base (multiple model, 16); namespace-scope and static member pointers to members are
  refused (S132, memptr-global.cpp); a cast of a floating literal to an integer type is folded modulo 2^N as cl
  folds it (`(int)3e9` b2d05e00h, `(unsigned short)1e10` e400h; IrGen value, Cast). Left (checked 2026-10-14, rows S8-S11): a negated literal
  (`(int)-2147483649.0`) was converted at run time (80000000h; cl /Od and /O2 give 7fffffff, as a printf argument
  and as a global initialiser), likewise unary plus, constant arithmetic, cast chains and const variables (fixed,
  rows S8/S9); a template instantiation with a member-pointer field was not refused when value-initialised
  (`H<P> h = H<P>();`: cl 0, cppparse garbage at -O0) or as a namespace-scope object (`H<P> gh;`: cl -1, cppparse
  0): both are S132 now (rows S1, S11; copies of one are still accepted, they move its bytes); the incomplete-class
  sizes held only for a class never completed in the unit (row S10, fixed).
- Statics in a member function of a local class: the mangled name lacks the enclosing function (cl nests
  it), so such a static is not shared with cl's copy; found by the review of 92a19bb (`int t4(int a) { struct L { static int f(int b) { static int s1 = b; return s1; } }; return L::f(a); }`).

## Code size against cl /O2 (analysis of the matrix sample, 2026-10-13)

mapcompare part C: the ten largest-ratio Matrix members are 1788 bytes against cl's 611 (2.93); the whole
class 1.85, all 95 functions both compilers emit 1.64. The causes, by extra bytes across the sample:

| | Cause | Bytes | Where | Size / risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | exception code in the body: constructed flags, destructors expanded in every landing pad, pads where nothing after construction can throw. Measured 2026-10-29 on the matrix sample at -O2 (e72478b): 1,401 of 5,867 instructions (24 %) lie in unwind, dtor and catch blocks, 84 unwind blocks, 210 dtor blocks (a flag test and a destructor call, the destructor often inlined there), 383 stores and 105 loads of the flag bytes. A1 done (2026-10-30): a call to a function with C linkage cannot throw (cl /EHsc), nor can the generator's own runtime entries (memcpy, memset, free, the statics' helpers): such calls are `nothrow` in the IR, get no exception edge (X86 computeEhEdges), and Optimizer::removeDeadUnwind drops a function's unwind region with its handler and the flag stores nothing reads when no instruction that may throw can run while a flag is set (IrGen clears a flag before it calls the destructor; the walk hands every throwing instruction's state to the handlers of the regions around it); IrGen runs it on every function with cleanups, the optimiser again after inlining. Own sources -O0 24,573,367 -> 24,197,248 bytes (-1.5 %), sample units -O0 -1.5 % and -O2 -2.5 %, front.cpp 205 -> 139 (-O0) / 86 (-O2) functions with a handler; matrix.cpp alone grew 3.5 %: destructors freed of their region (~Matrix) were inlined into every pad. A2 done (2026-10-30): inlineCalls skips the blocks the entry does not reach through branches (the pads, the dtor blocks, the catch bodies), as cl's `dtor$` and `catch$` funclets keep their calls: against the baseline before A1, matrix.cpp 99,854 -> 98,060 bytes (-1.8 %), solver.cpp -2.3 %, the sample's unwind/dtor/catch instructions 1,375 -> 1,049 (23.7 % -> 17.8 %), 38 -> 25 functions with a handler, mapcompare's largest ratio 15.8 -> 11.3 and cppparse smaller for 22 of 97 functions (18); sample units -O2 7,686,040 -> 7,451,371 (-3.1 %). Found with it, for F: the lowered-size measure counts a callee made of calls as small, so the chain ~Matrix -> ~vector -> destroyAll + free is inlined at every temporary's death (mtxLargeProductChecksum 731 -> 844 bytes against cl's 1,040): a call should cost more than 1 + its arguments in that measure. A3 and A4 done (2026-10-30): in the table model every object with a destructor has a try region of its own (IrGen::openRegion; the regions and the try blocks share one stack, left newest first by emitExits; a temporary dying under a later object closes and reopens that object's region, retireEntries) whose pad destroys it between cppparse_unwind_enter and leave (the exception parked meanwhile, terminate when one leaves the destructor) and rethrows, chaining through the ranges' nesting; no flag stored or tested except for an object built on one path only (an arm of ?:, a virtual base: its region opens at the join, its pad tests the flag) and in the setjmp model, which keeps the flags. A4: Optimizer::removeDeadRegions drops every region no throwing instruction reaches (an object alive only across C calls, a try block around code that cannot throw). The range table became one word an entry (code offset << 12 | pad number, a pad table after it; design-table-exceptions.md section 4), since a region per object means a range per state (the own sources: 12,373 -> 53,119 ranges). Against A2 (e547b1d), the same sources: own sources -O0 linked code 3,044,384 -> 2,775,792 bytes (-8.8 %), tables (.xdata) 128,868 -> 309,556, image 3,386,880 -> 3,299,328 (-2.6 %); sample units' objects -O0 8,284,221 -> 8,031,081 (-3.1 %), -O2 7,451,371 -> 7,194,428 (-3.4 %); matrix.cpp 98,060 -> 90,996 bytes (-7.2 %), its instructions 5,891 -> 5,195, of them in pads 1,049 -> 514, no function with a flag handler left; solver.cpp -2.6 %; mapcompare's median ratio against cl 1.8 -> 1.6, cppparse smaller for 23 of 97 functions; front.cpp linked code -O0 -5.5 %, -O2 -4.2 %. A5 done (2026-11-02): a pad whose cleanup is one direct destructor call (no flag to test, nothing to free, no array remainder, no guard to reset: IrGen::plainDestructor; 11,700 of the 13,303 pads in the own -O0 code) is one call, `cppparse_unwind_destroy(object, destructor)`, which enters, runs the destructor, leaves and rethrows as the four calls did (std::uncaught_exception() true in the destructor, terminate when an exception leaves it), followed by nothing: the IR's `unreachable` emits no code, and `int 3` closes a function whose code would otherwise end with the call, so that the return address the stack walk looks up stays inside the function. With it the dispatch stub of an object region, and of a try block whose first clause is catch (...), which was one jump to the pad (11,874 of the own -O0 code's 13,691 stubs), is gone: the range table names the pad itself (X86 m_padLabel). Against D9 (5154735) on the same sources: own sources -O0 linked code 2,598,304 -> 2,478,768 bytes (-4.6 %), unwind tables (.xdata) 251,140 -> 221,548 (-11.8 %: the range entries of the stubs are gone), image 3,063,296 -> 2,914,304 (-4.9 %), objects 23,809,480 -> 23,412,761; front.cpp linked -O0 94,352 -> 93,024, -O2 87,376 -> 86,432; matrix.cpp -O2 85,973 -> 83,794 bytes (-2.5 %), solver.cpp 80,419 -> 80,140; the objects of the sample units -O0 7,748,826 -> 7,707,897, -O2 6,964,605 -> 6,929,479; mapcompare as before (largest ratio 7.9, cppparse smaller for 31 of 97). Test 17: front.cpp's lean pads (122 at -O0, 89 at -O2) and no stub that is one jump; eh.ps1's ehPadDtor: a plain object, two, a temporary bound to a reference, a temporary of an arm of ?: (a flagged pad), each destructor sees uncaught_exception() true, throws and catches inside, and the exception goes on. Seen: 1,817 of the own -O0 regions keep the setjmp frames (X86 computeRegions fails on them: regions not nested the same way on every path), a size and speed item; in a function whose handlers take a value in a register the pad's two operands go through callee-saved registers and two moves (6 pads of front.cpp -O2). A6 done (2026-11-02): 15 of the own sources' functions kept the setjmp frames (X86 computeRegions failed on them), but they held 1,842 of the regions: `cppparseMain`, `X86Function::emitInst`, `emitVtables`, the report renderers, and each region there cost push_frame, setjmp (lea, xor, call, test, jne), pop_frame at every exit and a 272-byte frame slot, at run time a setjmp per region entry. One cause, found with an analyser of the IR listings (regions.py in the notes: it walks the blocks with the stack of open regions and reports the first block reached with two different stacks; it names exactly those 15): a `?:` nested in an arm of another opened its arms' deferred regions at the inner join, on that arm's path alone, while IrGen's stack held them on every path, so the tryends closing them at the end of the full expression popped other regions on the other arm's path (the loop conditions reached with different stacks and the tryend on an empty stack were the same thing further on). Now openDeferredRegions opens nothing while an outer conditional still defers: the outermost join opens every entry since its mark, inner arms' included, each guarded by its flag. Against D10 (495320e) on the same sources: own sources -O0 linked code 2,453,616 -> 2,299,968 bytes (-6.3 %), unwind tables (.xdata) 221,748 -> 250,052 (the ranges of those functions), image 2,889,728 -> 2,764,800 (-4.3 %), objects 23,429,333 -> 23,243,259; setjmp frames in the own -O0 code 1,842 -> 0; front.cpp (with nest6) linked -O0 94,048 -> 93,664, -O2 87,568 -> 87,200; matrix.cpp and solver.cpp unchanged (83,778, 80,140); the objects of the sample units -O0 7,706,517 -> 7,705,988, -O2 6,932,481 -> 6,931,968; mapcompare as before (largest ratio 7.9, cppparse smaller for 31 of 97). Over the whole plan since B7 (035b8d2): own -O0 linked code 2,818,784 -> 2,299,968 (-18.4 %), image 3,328,512 -> 2,764,800 (-16.9 %). Test 17: front.cpp gets nest6::pick (a `?:` nested in an arm of another, std::string temporaries in the arms: 5 setjmp frames before) and must have no cppparse_push_frame at either level; eh.ps1's ehNestedCond builds temporaries in nested arms with a constructor that throws in either arm or a throw after the join, and logs constructions and destructions as cl does. A7 done (2026-11-02): the temporaries of a condition live to the end of its full expression (12.2/3). IrGen::condition destroyed each operand's before branching, which cl does not: the condition of `?:` lost its temporary before the arms (a3's `mMn.N`), and in `if (f(T()) && g(T()))` the first operand's died before the second ran (`aAbB.` for cl's `abBA.`); a condition of one operand, and loops with one, were right already. Now condition(e, t, f, fullExpr) lowers the tree (conditionTree) without ending temporaries; an operand after the first branch of `&&` or `||` runs on one path only, so its temporaries are flagged (m_deferRegions, as an arm's of `?:`) and marked guarded; a statement's condition (fullExpr) with temporaries left after the tree destroys them in one exit block both targets are reached through, the branch outcome kept in a bool meanwhile (redirectBranches turns the tree's branches to the targets into branches to two blocks that store it; a condition of one operand still ends its temporaries before its own branch, so nothing changes for the common case); the condition of `?:` and of the value of `&&` / `||` leaves its temporaries to the enclosing full expression (the `?:` join opens their regions with the arms', flaggedAtStart taken before the condition), and a scalar arm's temporaries stay too, guarded by their flags, instead of dying at the arm's end. Unwinding order fixed with it (pre-existing for an arm's temporary): a flagged object had no region until the join, so the pads of the regioned objects below it ran first and the function's unwind handler destroyed it last; now every cleanup opens its region at once, an arm or a later operand closes the regions of its temporaries before the join or its branch (suspendRegions: tryends, the entries deferred again) and the join reopens them under their flags, so the unwinding order follows the nesting and every path reaches a join with the same regions (A6's guard against inner joins went: the outer arm's end suspends what an inner join reopened); a pad opened at creation needs no flag test and takes the lean call, one opened at a join tests the flag (m_pads carries it). One window stays, an operand's temporary suspended in the condition of `?:` or in the value of `&&` / `||` until the enclosing join or the full expression's end: it goes on the lists of the objects whose regions are open (Cleanup::newer) and their pads destroy it first (a first version listed every flagged entry and grew the own -O0 code 3 %). Against A6 (7eac32d) on the same sources, the price of the longer lifetimes (regions and pads for the temporaries of arms and later operands, the exit blocks of statement conditions): own sources -O0 linked code 2,303,536 -> 2,317,888 bytes (+0.6 %), unwind tables 250,228 -> 254,820, image 2,768,384 -> 2,787,328 (+0.7 %), objects 23,248,352 -> 23,279,316; front.cpp (with cond7) linked -O0 94,640 -> 94,896, -O2 88,080 -> 88,272; matrix.cpp -O2 83,778 -> 84,337, solver.cpp unchanged; the objects of the sample units -O0 7,710,578 -> 7,711,611, -O2 6,935,694 -> 6,936,798; mapcompare as before (largest ratio 7.9, cppparse smaller for 31 of 97); every own and sample function in the table model with consistent region stacks (regions.py over 45,543 functions), no setjmp frame. Test 17: front.cpp's cond7 prints the construction and destruction log of five conditions, compared with cl's build; eh.ps1's ehCondTemps does the same with throws from a second operand, from an arm and from the statement; probe a3 is now equal to cl in full. Row A is complete | ~1700 | IrGen registerCleanup, runCleanup, the unwind region (design-table-exceptions.md section 7); Optimizer inlineCalls; X86 computeEhEdges, setRegions | large / medium |
| B | register allocator: float pool only callee-saved xmm6-13, rax/rcx/rdx not in the pool, intervals ending at a call treated as crossing it, spills not weighted by loop depth, no memory operands, copies not coalesced. Step B1 done (2026-10-29): an interval a call only reads (an argument at its last use) or only defines (its result) may use r8-r11 (Interval::crosses from the clobber positions and the liveness at a block's end; throw, catch and setjmp try keep the inclusive rule), a call's result may take the register of an argument whose last use is that call, the value returned right after the function's unwind nop is computed in rax, and emitCall stages the register arguments and an indirect target as a parallel move (a cycle through rax). At -O0 nearly every function with a call had pushed rbx for its arguments and results (12,006 of the own sources' 16,409 functions). Own sources + libcpp at -O0: objects 25,066,911 -> 24,859,469 bytes (-0.8 %); sample-lib + front.cpp -O0 -0.9 %, -O2 -0.7 %. front.cpp callregs::, test 17 reads the listing. Step B2 done (2026-10-29): a value whose last use is a call's register argument is computed in that argument's register when nothing before the call writes it (hintArguments, holdsArgReg: writesArgReg's rcx/rdx sequences, the defining instruction included, the parameters' reads at the start, no other value in the register), which removes the copies before calls (161,246 at -O0 in the own sources); own sources -O0 objects 24,859,469 -> 24,451,968 (-1.6 %, -2.5 % against a6b0ef8), sample units -O0 -1.0 %, -O2 -0.4 %; front.cpp callregs::straight. Step B3 done (2026-10-29): a call's result read once, soon after the call (only constants, addresses and loads into registers between), by an instruction that takes it before writing rax (a store of the value, conv, neg, ret, branch, copy, a load through it, the first operand of an arithmetic or a comparison; not a call argument) stays in rax (resultsInRax, after the last allocation); own sources -O0 objects 24,488,378 -> 24,427,008 (-0.25 % against B2 on the same sources), sample units -O0 -0.2 %, -O2 -0.1 %; front.cpp callregs::stored, stored2. Step B4 done (2026-10-30): a value takes over the register of an operand whose last use is the instruction defining it (a copy, so the phi copies after SSA; a binary or unary operation, so the two-address forms "mov r9, rcx; add r9, rdx"; a conversion within the class, an address computation, a load): the dying interval leaves the active list without freeing its register (allocate). Against dbdbc2b: matrix.cpp 5,195 -> 5,071 instructions (-2.4 %), its register-to-register moves 766 -> 702, mapcompare's median ratio 1.6 -> 1.5; front.cpp -O0 52,410 -> 51,092 instructions (moves 5,560 -> 4,535), -O2 45,142 -> 44,479, linked code -2.3 % / -1.4 %; own sources' objects -0.3 %, sample units -0.5 % / -0.4 %. Of the moves left at -O2, 55 % stage call arguments from callee-saved registers (as cl's code does) and 14 % return a value kept in one; the rest are mostly three-operand needs (cl uses lea for an add whose operand lives on: D) and spill traffic. Step B6 done (2026-10-30): xmm3 and xmm2 join the float pool as volatile registers (fregName indexes 8 and 9; for a value that crosses no call, throw or try operation and starts after the last read of the parameter their register carries; never saved), the floating-point operation's second-operand scratch moved from xmm2 to xmm5, and emitCall stages register-resident floating-point arguments as a parallel move (fregOf, a cycle through xmm4). Against 20b130a: matrix.cpp 5,071 -> 4,973 instructions, its saved xmm registers 64 -> 22, object -2.0 %, solver.cpp 81 -> 36 saves; front.cpp -O0 saves 232 -> 37, -O2 167 -> 31, linked code -1.4 % / -1.6 %; sample units' objects -O0 -0.1 %, -O2 -0.6 %; mapcompare: cppparse smaller than cl for 27 of 97 functions. Seen: a parameter in xmm0 or xmm1 is still copied out at entry (analyzeParams keeps integer parameters in place only), every call copies its floating-point arguments to the integer registers as for a variadic callee (D: IrGen knows the callee's variadic-ness), a floating-point result returned right after its call goes through xmm3 (returnDirect handles integers only). Step B7 done (2026-10-30): every interval carries a weight (its definitions and uses, 1, 10, 100 or 1000 by the loop depth of their block, Optimizer::naturalLoops in computeIntervals) and a spill takes the value with the lowest weight per instruction of its life, the new one included (density; ties to the longest-living, as before). The number of spills stays (a conflict spills one value either way): matrix.cpp 88,455 -> 88,480 bytes, solver.cpp 83,031 -> 82,869, front.cpp -O2 941,269 -> 940,594 (linked code -0.45 %), -O0 unchanged, sample units -O2 -0.2 %; a step for speed more than size. B is done but for memory operands, which belong to D's instruction selection (at -O0 every operand is loaded into a register first; D's widths decide the forms); a writesRax predicate per instruction (as writesArgReg for rcx/rdx) would let any instruction stand between a call and the reader of its result. Next big -O0 item is D's constant fold: 109,325 `const` instructions in the own sources' listings (58 % feed a storelocal, then conv, cmp, bin, call arguments, ret), each a register and an instruction that an immediate operand would replace | ~1100 | X86.cpp allocate, coalesce, hintArguments, resultsInRax, emitCall | medium / medium |
| C | `throw std::invalid_argument("...")` builds a heap std::string temporary; cl uses the `const char*` constructor and `_CxxThrowException` (35 bytes). Done (2026-10-29): logic_error and runtime_error hold a copy of the message made and freed by two inline functions no caller expands (`__message_copy`, `__message_free`), and every class has the `const char*` constructor cl's headers have beside the one from a string; a thrown literal costs the constructor's few instructions and a call. Matrix sample -O2: operator+= 359 -> 234 bytes, maxAbsDiff 473 -> 338, operator* 780 -> 705, objects -2.3 %; the sample units' code -0.3 % while their object files grow 0.1 % (the new COMDAT symbols and unwind records). Probe c1.cpp and front.cpp stdexc:: print what cl prints; matrix.ps1's clang strict C++98 check passes | ~600 | include/stdexcept | small / low |
| D | instruction selection: base+index*8 addressing, `add r,8` pointer steps, `xorps` for 0.0, setcc and jump threading for bools, redundant movsxd (widen int counters), FP-to-integer copies only for variadic callees, hot slots within rbp-128. Step D1 done (2026-10-30): an integer of 1, 2 or 4 bytes lives in the low 32 bits of its register, the upper 32 unspecified (a 4-byte load is a plain mov r32, a 1- or 2-byte load promotes to 32 bits by its signedness with movsx/movzx r32), and Bin, Un, Copy and Conv use the 32-bit forms when the type is at most 4 bytes; the extension to 64 bits is emitted only where 64 bits are consumed: Conv to an 8-byte type, an unsigned int feeding a float conversion, and AddrIndex's index (movsxd or mov r32 into a scratch register ahead of the lea/imul). A 4-byte call result stays as the callee left it in eax, 4-byte parameters are used in place. front.cpp -O2 43,870 -> 41,954 instructions (-4.4 %, movsxd 3,198 -> 407), -O0 50,810 -> 49,121 (-3.3 %, movsxd 3,625 -> 642), its linked code -3.8 % / -4.1 %; matrix.cpp 4,976 -> 4,895 instructions, solver.cpp 4,161 -> 4,064; the own sources' linked code at -O0 2,818,784 -> 2,788,960 (-1.1 %); the sample units' objects -O0 -0.3 %, -O2 -0.4 %; mapcompare's median ratio against cl 1.5 -> 1.3, the largest 10.0 -> 9.7. Test 17: coal::sum3 has no movsxd at either level. Left in D: immediates at -O0 (the 109,325 const instructions, D2), FP-to-integer copies only for variadic callees (D3), lea for three-operand adds and `add r,8` steps (D4), xorps for 0.0, setcc and jump threading for bools, hot slots within rbp-128. Step D2 done (2026-10-30): at -O0 a `const` defined once and read once becomes its reader's immediate operand before the lowering (constantsIntoOperands, on a copy of the function in X86Module::codeFor, so the IR listings stay as generated): a store's value, the second operand of an arithmetic instruction or a comparison (a commutative operation or a comparison with the constant first gets its operands exchanged), a call argument, a returned value; a conversion of one to an integer or pointer type folds to a constant by constantFold's rules and goes on. The own sources' listings at -O0: 1,042,275 -> 978,462 instructions (-6.1 %), const instructions 53,927 -> 2,822, mov reg, imm 38,617 -> 16,623; their linked code 2,797,024 -> 2,691,824 (-3.8 %), objects -0.9 %; front.cpp -O0 49,121 -> 46,606 instructions (-5.1 %), linked code 103,568 -> 99,328 (-4.1 %); the sample units' objects -O0 -1.0 %. At -O2 the optimiser's constant propagation had done it already; there only a negative constant of a type up to 4 bytes now loads through the 32-bit register (mov r32, imm, like a non-negative one: solver.cpp two instructions, front.cpp's linked code 91,712 -> 91,632). Step D3 done (2026-10-30): a floating-point argument is copied into its integer register (movq) only for a variadic callee: IrGen marks a call variadic from the callee's function type (a direct call, a call through a pointer to function or to member; a callee of unknown type counts as variadic), the IR prints it, and emitCall's two movq sites read the mark. matrix.cpp -O2 movq 20 -> 0 (88,238 -> 88,142 bytes), solver.cpp 30 -> 0 (82,569 -> 82,417); front.cpp -O0 46,606 -> 46,412 instructions (movq 243 -> 49, printf's), linked code 99,328 -> 98,384 (-1.0 %), -O2 41,954 -> 41,907 (movq 97 -> 50), 91,632 -> 91,440; the own sources (few doubles) linked code 2,692,160 -> 2,691,216. Step D4 done (2026-10-30): an addrindex with a constant index puts the offset in the instruction (lea rd, [base+off]; add rd, off when the result takes the base's register; a huge or an unsigned negative index still goes through a register), writesArgReg knows it leaves rcx alone (a stepped pointer parameter stays in place: lea rax, [rcx+12]; ret), and D2's folding places a constant index at -O0 too; an add or a subtraction of an immediate whose result goes into another register is one lea (lea rd, [a+-imm]), as is an add of two registers into a third (lea rd, [a+b]; the low 32 bits of the sum are the 32-bit result). front.cpp -O0 46,491 -> 45,088 instructions (-3.0 %), linked code 98,704 -> 96,080 (-2.7 %), -O2 41,955 -> 40,392 (-3.7 %), 91,664 -> 88,816 (-3.1 %); matrix.cpp 4,875 -> 4,749 instructions, solver.cpp 4,034 -> 3,870 (-4.1 %); the sample units' objects -O0 -0.6 %, -O2 -0.7 %; the own sources -0.9 % instructions, linked code 2,696,800 -> 2,688,016. Test 17: lea4::step is lea rax, [p+12] at both levels, lea4::sum adds with a lea. Step D5 done (2026-10-30): the frame in order of use: computeIntervals weighs every local like a temporary (its loads, stores and address-takings, 1, 10, 100 or 1000 by loop depth) and layoutFrame lays out the locals and the spill slots by weight per 8 bytes, the most used nearest rbp, where a byte of displacement reaches (a variadic function's parameters stay in their home slots; the saved xmm registers, the setjmp frames and the throw slot follow as before). Frame references needing four bytes of displacement: the own sources at -O0 89,568 -> 74,682 (29 % -> 24 % of 309,048), linked code 2,690,816 -> 2,649,328 (-1.5 %); front.cpp -O0 1,723 -> 1,285, linked code 96,576 -> 95,200 (-1.4 %), -O2 2,698 -> 1,606, 89,024 -> 87,232 (-2.0 %); matrix.cpp 465 -> 248 (87,619 -> 86,976 bytes), solver.cpp 463 -> 284 (81,752 -> 81,190); the sample units' objects -O0 -0.2 %, -O2 -0.4 %; the instruction counts do not change. Test 17: hot5::many at -O0 keeps its scalars within 128 bytes of rbp although its array is declared first. Seen: `int a[40] = { 0 }` at -O0 is a memset, one store and 39 dead `lea r8, [rbx+k]` (the addresses IrGen emits for the elements the memset already covers; -O2's dead-code elimination removes them). Step D6 done (2026-10-30): +0.0 (every bit clear, so of either precision) clears its register with xorps instead of loading the constant, as an instruction's own constant (FConst) and as an operand use() materialises (FImm; xorps leaves the flags alone, so it may stand between a comparison and its branch): front.cpp -O0 21 loads -> 0, linked code 95,200 -> 95,040, -O2 31 -> 0, 87,232 -> 87,024; matrix.cpp 27 -> 0 (86,976 -> 86,512 bytes), solver.cpp 25 -> 0 (81,190 -> 80,779); the own sources' 13 loads -> 0 (linked code 2,650,192 -> 2,650,144); the sample units' objects -O0 7,811,229 -> 7,809,132, -O2 7,002,537 -> 6,998,766. Test 17: front.cpp never loads the zero constant, xorps at -O2. Step D7 done (2026-10-30): at -O0 a load of a local that follows its store in the same block takes the stored value (the register, or the constant: the load becomes a constant and D2's folding goes on) when no call, throw or try operation lies between, as the optimiser's store-to-load forwarding does from -O1 on (constantsIntoOperands, on the back end's copy; the store stays, so the debugger sees the variable); a volatile local is marked address-taken by IrGen::newLocal, so neither this nor -O1's forwarding and SSA promotion touches it (front.cpp's volatile test variables now live in memory at -O2: lit::show +63 instructions). Own sources -O0 973,844 -> 971,116 instructions, linked code 2,653,936 -> 2,646,720 (-0.3 %); front.cpp -O0 linked code 95,040 -> 94,512, -O2 87,024 -> 87,424 (the volatile variables); the sample units' objects -O0 7,809,132 -> 7,804,298, -O2 6,998,766 -> 7,000,294; matrix and solver unchanged. Test 17: fwd7::chain at -O0 never reloads t, fwd7::vol reloads its volatile v after each store. Seen: of the store-then-reload pairs left in the own sources' -O0 listings, 3,622 cross a `?:` join (both arms store the same temporary: a phi would be needed), 867 the cleanup.cont edge after an object's region opens (one predecessor: the known values could flow along it), ~680 are locals whose address is taken for a by-reference argument (safe to forward while no call or store through a pointer lies between). Step D8 done (2026-10-30): the blocks are laid out in groups (X86Function::layoutBlocks): the entry's group (the blocks its branches reach, in their order), then the exit code, then each landing pad or handler with what it alone reaches, so that the `br cont` over the pad IrGen places between a region's opening block and its continuation falls through, a return at the end of the ordinary code falls into the exit, and the cold code sits after it (m_order drives the linear order of the intervals and the emission, m_next the fall-through tests). Jumps hopping over a pad in front.cpp 169 -> 0 at -O0, 12 -> 0 at -O2; front.cpp linked code -O0 94,736 -> 94,144 (-0.6 %), -O2 87,584 -> 87,152 (-0.5 %); matrix.cpp 4,749 -> 4,713 instructions (86,512 -> 86,154 bytes), solver.cpp 3,870 -> 3,857 (80,779 -> 80,669); the own sources' -O0 listings 971,455 -> 957,093 instructions (-1.5 %, jumps 65,717 -> 54,448), their linked code 2,648,208 -> 2,605,680 (-1.6 %), the unwind tables 295,748 -> 265,820 (a region's ranges no longer split around its pad), image 3,158,016 -> 3,085,312 (-2.3 %); the sample units' objects -O0 7,804,298 -> 7,770,919, -O2 7,000,294 -> 6,983,206. Test 17: no jump hops over a landing pad at either level. Seen: IrGen emits blocks nothing branches to (a loop's break block without a break statement), dead code that only -O1's branch folding removes; of the own -O0 jumps 22,853 are the second jump of a condbr whose targets are both out of line, which a layout that chains a branch's successor next would halve. Step D9 done (2026-10-30): within a group the blocks go in chains: after a block, one of its branch targets still to place, by preference the one that only this block enters (the next operand of `&&` or `||`, a then-block), then the one IrGen made right after it, then the true target; a chain that ends continues with the lowest-numbered block left; the intervals keep the blocks' own order (a register assignment made for one linear order holds for any emission order of the same blocks; the first try fed the chain order to computeIntervals, whose loop heuristic then extended live ranges and grew the matrix sample by 27 instructions). front.cpp jumps -O0 916 -> 638, -O2 1,261 -> 938 (linked code 94,384 -> 94,352, 87,344 -> 87,376); matrix.cpp 4,713 -> 4,677 instructions (86,154 -> 85,973 bytes), solver.cpp 3,857 -> 3,813 (80,669 -> 80,419); the own sources' -O0 listings 957,225 -> 931,071 instructions (-2.7 %; jumps 54,471 -> 24,937, the second jumps of condbrs 20,419 -> 462), their linked code 2,606,208 -> 2,594,944 (-0.4 %: the jumps left reach further), the unwind tables 265,820 -> 250,708, image 3,085,824 -> 3,059,712 (-0.8 %); the sample units' objects -O0 7,771,948 -> 7,748,826, -O2 6,984,040 -> 6,964,605. Test 17: in chain9::both no conditional jump is followed by an unconditional one. Step D10 done (2026-11-02): measured first, the item was not what the row said: no setcc is followed by a test and a branch in the own -O0 code (IrGen::condition branches `&&`, `||` and `!` directly; the 1,009 setcc are materialised bools, 684 of them returned, the rest stored), what costs is a bool tested after a zero-extension: 6,217 times `call; movzx eax, al; test eax, eax` and 5,823 times `movzx r, byte ptr [x]; test r, r` (4,018 locals, 1,805 members and globals), both inherent to the width convention (D1: a narrow value lives in the low 32 bits). Now a 1-byte value read once, by the conditional branch right after its definition, is tested where it is (X86 m_byteTest, found with the fused comparisons): a call's result in its byte register (`test al, al`, no extension), a local, a member or a global in memory (`cmp byte ptr [x], 0`, the load emits nothing and the temporary gets no register; the branch reads the address of a load from memory, so the interval of that address and writesArgReg count the branch as the load), a parameter still in its register in that register's byte. Against A5 (b6d61c4) on the same sources: own sources -O0 linked code 2,487,760 -> 2,453,600 bytes (-1.4 %), image 2,923,520 -> 2,889,728 (-1.2 %), objects 23,469,203 -> 23,429,309; front.cpp linked -O0 93,024 -> 92,896, -O2 86,432 -> 86,400; matrix.cpp -O2 83,794 -> 83,778 bytes, solver.cpp 80,140 as before; the objects of the sample units -O0 7,707,897 -> 7,701,842, -O2 6,929,479 -> 6,926,978; mapcompare as before (largest ratio 7.9, cppparse smaller for 31 of 97). Over the whole plan since B7 (035b8d2): own -O0 linked code 2,818,784 -> 2,453,600 (-13.0 %), image 3,328,512 -> 2,889,728 (-13.2 %); front.cpp linked -O2 95,680 -> 86,400 (-9.7 %), -O0 107,632 -> 92,896 (-13.7 %). Test 17: front.cpp has no call result zero-extended for a test at either level, 62 bytes compared in memory and 8 tested in a byte register at -O0 (45 and 6 at -O2); the probe d10.cpp (bool and char results, locals, parameters in registers and in memory, a member through a pointer, a global, negative chars, the same values used as integers) prints what cl prints at -O0 and -O2. Step D11 done (2026-11-03): the dead code IrGen leaves at -O0, measured on the own -O0 IR listings (deadcode.py in the notes): 177 blocks no branch or region reaches (the code after a return, the end of a switch every case returns from), 472 instructions, and 3,537 side-effect-free instructions whose result nothing reads (2,304 loads of locals, mostly `this` loaded for a member access that uses another copy, 521 field addresses, 237 constants, 210 addresses of locals, 125 of globals; the optimiser removes them from -O1 on). Now the back end's pre-pass ends with dropUnreadValues: a value nothing reads, computed without a side effect (an address, a constant, a copy, an arithmetic result other than a division, a comparison, a conversion, a load of a local whose address nothing holds, so never a volatile one), goes, and what fed it alone with it; loads through pointers and of globals stay, the IR not telling a volatile one apart. And layoutBlocks places the entry's group and the handlers of the regions of placed blocks alone: a block nothing reaches is not emitted, nor is a dispatch stub of a region in one. Against the CodeView fix (49d295d) on the same sources: own sources -O0 instructions emitted 831,848 -> 827,776 (-0.5 %), objects 23,314,980 -> 23,265,876, linked code 2,320,704 -> 2,318,592 (-0.1 %: most of the dropped values sat in template members the linker discards, and functions are 16-byte aligned), image 2,789,888 -> 2,787,840; front.cpp (with unread8) linked -O0 95,136 -> 94,048 (-1.1 %), -O2 unchanged (88,448: the optimiser removed them already), as are matrix.cpp and solver.cpp; the objects of the sample units -O0 7,722,635 -> 7,709,918, -O2 unchanged; mapcompare as before (largest ratio 7.9, cppparse smaller for 31 of 97); region stacks consistent and no setjmp frame over 45,559 own and sample functions. Test 17: front.cpp has no block labelled dead at either level, unread8::f (an expression statement `p->a;`, an unused parameter k) never touches edx, unread8::g (a switch every case returns from) has no switch.end block. Row D is complete | ~400 | X86.cpp, Ssa/Optimizer | small each / low |
| E | no NRVO and no elision of a copy from a returned temporary (transpose, operator*, `Matrix p = a*b`); NRVO done 2026-10-13 (row 14d), a returned temporary was already built in place | ~320 | IrGen (return slot, destination address) | medium / medium |
| F | inlining thresholds counted on unoptimised IR (sameSize 26 instructions for 20 bytes). Done (2026-10-29): inlineCalls measures a callee by loweredSize, what the SSA passes and the lowering leave of it (no loads and stores of promotable locals, nops, unconditional branches or field addresses; a call with its arguments): sameSize 26 -> 11. The limits re-set for it: 6 everywhere and 16 in a loop (8 and 24 on the new measure inlined more: the sample units +1.2 %, the matrix sample's code +17 %), -O3's second round 24. Sample units -O2 7,649,532 -> 7,624,089 (-0.3 %), matrix sample .text 23,682 -> 23,362 (-1.4 %), class Matrix 2,950 -> 2,896 bytes against cl's 1,872. front.cpp inl::, test 17 reads the -O2 listing | ~150 | Optimizer.cpp inlineCalls, loweredSize | small / low |
| G | rbp frame in leaf functions, no tail calls (`~Matrix`: cl `add rcx,8; jmp _Tidy`) | ~100 | X86.cpp | small / low. Measured 2026-10-30 (after D9): a leaf with nothing in memory is frameless already (decideFrame); the calls in tail position are 21 of 599 in matrix.cpp, 14 of 224 in solver.cpp, 155 of 3,234 in front.cpp -O2 and 260 of 4,419 at -O0, and a tail jump saves the `ret` alone (one byte each, the epilogue moves before the jump), so the row is worth under 0.1 % of code: left as is |
| H | `fabs` called instead of `andpd`, `std::max(const&)` kept through memory after inlining | ~70 | Optimizer | small / low. Measured 2026-10-30: 5 calls of fabs in matrix.cpp, 16 in solver.cpp, 6 in the own sources, none in front.cpp; `andpd` with a 16-byte mask constant is no smaller than the call (a speed item); `std::max(const&)` through memory is the inliner's by-reference parameters (an optimiser item for -O2 only): left as is |

Order: C and F (small, low risk), then B, then A, then D, E, G, H. Expected Matrix members ratio: about 1.65
after A+B+C, about 1.15-1.3 after A-F; `~Matrix` needs G and `index`/`sameSize` need D to reach 1.25; the
free functions depend mostly on A. Note: cl moves cleanup and catch code into `dtor$`/`catch$` symbols, which
mapcompare does not add to the function (mtxSingularThrows is 465 bytes with them, not 207).

Two qualification conversions of one argument are ranked as 13.3.3.2/3 ranks them, the target whose cv-qualification signature is a proper subset of the other's first, and of two reference bindings to one type the less qualified (compareQualifications): without it the conversion accepted would have made `pick(int* const*)` and `pick(const int* const*)` ambiguous for an `int**` (cl takes the first), and `cv(const int*)` against `cv(const volatile int*)` or `cr(const int&)` against `cr(const volatile int&)` were ambiguous already. A probe of six element sizes (1, 2, 4, 8, 12 and 16 bytes) had an `idiv` in all six functions at -O0 and in five at -O2 (the optimiser folded the division by 1 only); now in one, the 12-byte one, at both levels, as cl /Od; 200 million char-pointer differences take 526 ms at -O0 (2137 ms before; cl /Od 333 ms; -O2 190 ms before and after); the four builds print the same. front.cpp `ptrdiff::` against cl at -O0 and -O2, and test 17 reads the -O0 listing: no `idiv` in `ptrdiff::bytes` and `ptrdiff::ints`, one in `ptrdiff::t12s`. ## Review of 2026-11-05 (Fable 5.1): release 6.0 against its two claims, strict C++98 and self-hosting

Checked on 5a23729 (tag v6.0) in a clean worktree: `selfhost.ps1` passes at -O0 (4 min) and at -O2 (10 min; 39 of 39
units and libcpp identical, generation two identical at both levels), `build-cppparse.ps1 -Exe out\selfhost\cppparse-self.exe`
with the -O2 self-built compiler passes all 18 groups (340 checks), `crc32.ps1` matches (279 files). The own sources under
clang (`VC\Tools\Llvm\x64\bin\clang++ --target=x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -std=c++98 -pedantic -fsyntax-only
-fno-ms-compatibility -fno-delayed-template-parsing -nostdinc -nostdinc++ -I<each source folder> -Iinclude -Iplatform`):
0 errors, 4,055 warnings over the 39 units (rows P4, P5, P7, P8, P9). The probes of rows P11 to P18 (kept out of the
repository) ran with the release binary, with `cl /permissive- /Zc:trigraphs /X` and with `clang++ -std=c++98
-pedantic-errors`. The review changed no code; the rows record what is left.

| # | Case | State |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | self-hosting boundary: `runtime/cppparse_rt.cpp` is compiled by cl in every self-host link (selfhost.ps1), and cppparse cannot compile it: it needs the SDK's windows.h and intrin.h (CONTEXT, RtlVirtualUnwind, RtlLookupFunctionEntry; `platform/windows.h` declares none of them) and `__declspec(thread)`, which the parser drops (CxxParser.cpp keeps align and noreturn only); its header comment says "Strict C++98 (C subset)". The linker and libcmt/libucrt/libvcruntime (`__RTtypeid`, `__RTDynamicCast`, `_Init_thread_*`) are MSVC's as well | open: the compiler, libcpp and the COFF writer self-host; the runtime, the C library and the linker do not. To self-compile the runtime: thread-local storage in the compiler (`__declspec(thread)`, or TLS slots through the platform header), the unwind declarations in `platform/windows.h`, the runtime's comment corrected. The design, in three levels (own runtime, GRIDE's link, GRIDE's runtime), is docs/design-selfhost.md (2026-11-19: independently reviewed, the findings folded in as its section 10; the user's go-ahead of 2026-11-20 on the recommended answers to the ten questions of its section 7). **Stage 0a done (2026-11-20; scripts, nothing in the compiler), one of its exit tests open on GRIDE.** What holds: `selfhost.ps1 -Linker gride` links the self-host with GRIDE's link and `/timestamp:0` in folders of its own (Microsoft's link stays the default of every script); generation two's 47 objects, listings and libcpp are identical as before, and the two exes are byte-identical, 3,140,096 bytes at -O0 and 9,135,616 at -O2. harness.ps1 has four checks more: `selfhost-gride-O0` and `selfhost-gride-O2` (each level's objects linked again by GRIDE's link, the exes compared; seconds), `suite-self-gride` (every test group under the GRIDE-linked -O2 compiler) and `replace` (replace.ps1); they pass in the table model and under `-Eh cl`, and the checks of before pass unchanged. A check that needs GRIDE is `SKIPPED` by name where GRIDE is not found (`GRIDE_ROOT`, else My Documents\GRIDE3, else the installed location), which is not a pass and fails under `-RequireGride`; each logs the SHA-256 of the GRIDE binaries it used. What is open: the plan's first work, a search kind for the replaceable definitions, is no fix: Microsoft's link honours NOLIBRARY only while no other object names the function (measured, the design's section 10; ALIAS stays and no object changes), so under GRIDE's link the C runtime's `operator new` still takes libcpp's place wherever a program defines none, which the checks print and count (20 of replace.ps1's cases in the table model, 17 in cl's; the GRIDE-linked compiler's own `operator new` is libcmt's) until GRIDE's link treats a weak external of the alias kind as link.exe does: request G7, exit test `replace.ps1 -Strict`. | P9 | `<vector>` moves and erases elements bytewise (`memcpy` in grow, `memmove` in erase: vector:147, 156, 179; "every type stored here must tolerate that") where 23.2.4 copy-constructs and assigns; wrong for a type that holds a pointer into itself or has an observable copy constructor or assignment (clang -Wnontrivial-memcall at 220 instantiations over the own sources) | fixed (2026-11-12): `<vector>` copy-constructs its elements into a new buffer and destroys the old ones (push_back, insert, reserve, resize, the constructors), insert in place copy-constructs what lands past the old end and assigns the rest, erase assigns the later elements down and destroys the last ones (23.2.4.3, 23.2.4.4); three private members do it (`rebuild`, `spread`, `close`), each in two overloads chosen at compile time by `__is_bytewise<T>` of `<__traits>`: the arithmetic types and the pointers keep memcpy and memmove, every other type (an enumeration too, which C++98 cannot tell from a class) takes the general path. A reallocation builds the new buffer before it touches the old one, so a throwing copy has no effect (push_back, 23.1/10; reserve; insert and resize when they reallocate) and the value may be an element of the vector; in place a throwing copy leaves valid elements; a constructor that throws leaves nothing behind (the copy constructor leaked the elements it had built). The storage comes from `::operator new` and goes to `::operator delete` (it was malloc, unchecked), so bad_alloc is the library's and no `throw` is inlined. `examples/sample-lib/vector.cpp` (test 15, against cl's build with its own headers at -O0 and -O2, 251 lines): Self (a pointer into itself), Cnt (counted, knows raw and destroyed storage), int, double and int* through push_back, reserve, insert (one, n, a range; in place and reallocating; the value an element), erase, pop_back, resize, swap, assign, operator=, clear and a copy that throws. The counts of constructions, copies, assignments and destructions equal cl's on every step but the ones the standard leaves open and cl does otherwise, where only the elements, the number of living objects and the broken ones are compared: growth from nothing (cl grows by half, this library doubles), insert of a range in place (cl destroys and constructs where this library assigns), assign and operator= (cl assigns over the elements it has; C++98 23.2.4.1 says erase and insert, which this library does). strict98.ps1 allows nothing but the two typedef pairs now (146 -Wc++11-long-long). The cost, measured on the self-built compiler (whose own sources use this <vector>; selfhost.ps1, the machine busy with another harness): cppparse-self.exe 2,831,360 to 2,909,184 bytes at -O0 (+2.7 %) and 8,647,680 to 8,796,672 at -O2 (+1.7 %); the self-built compiler compiling the 39 sources and libcpp (stage 2) 95 to 101 s at -O0 and 221 to 227 s at -O2. The other containers do not move elements bytewise: `<deque>`, `<list>`, `__tree`, `<valarray>` copy and assign; `<string>`, the streams and `<strstream>` use memcpy and memmove on characters only, which 21.1.1 defines traits::copy and traits::move to be for char and wchar_t (a user's char_traits::copy is not called: basic_string copies the characters itself; no row) |

In literals 2.2/2 is an error too (clang -std=c++98 agrees; cl applies C++11's rule and accepts `"\u0041"`). Literals against cl: a narrow one takes the byte of code page 1252 (the 27 characters at 0x80 to 0x9F as well: `"\u20ac"` is 80h), `?` for a character the page lacks and two for one beyond 16 bits; a narrow piece joined to a wide literal goes through the code page first (`L"a" "\u0100"` ends in `?`); `L'\U0001F600'` is the first surrogate (was the low 16 bits); in a multicharacter constant a universal character name is no escape sequence of cl's rule and is always shifted in; in an `#if` a narrow one is its UTF-8 bytes (`'\u00e9' == 0xC3A9`, cl's preprocessor). `examples/sample-front/ucn.cpp` (test 17: against cl at -O0 and -O2, cppparse's object linked with cl's object of the UCN_OTHER half; `-E` keeps the spelling) and `ucn-refused.cpp` (12 errors, the skipped group and the valid line silent). | R5 | an explicit specialisation declared after a use that instantiated the template for the same arguments (14.7.3/6; cl C2908, clang "after instantiation") was accepted: `template <class T> struct A { int k() { return 1; } }; A<int> a; template <> struct A<int> { int k() { return 2; } };` compiled, `a.k()` 1; a use in a function body written before the specialisation silently took the specialisation (bodies are analysed after the unit's declarations); `template <> int A<int>::s = 2;` of a static data member was ignored (the template's value stayed); and a class only named before its specialisation (`A<int>* p;`, valid) kept the template's instantiation for the types made from that name (S105 "cannot convert A<int>* to A<int>*", or the template's layout under a typedef) | fixed (2026-11-12): S110 "explicit specialisation of 'X' after its use [in 'f']: it has already been instantiated (cl C2908)" for class templates, function templates, member functions and static data members of class templates. Two checks (Sema::specializationUse): in the declaration pass a use marks the implicit instantiation (`Symbol::instantiationUsed`: an object or member of the class type, a base, sizeof, new, a qualifier `A<int>::`, a member access, an expression of the class type; a function or static member referred to), and a specialisation declared afterwards is refused (explicitSpecialization, declareFunctionSpecialization, declareStaticMemberSpecialization); in a body written in the unit, a specialisation whose place among the unit's (`Symbol::specOrder`) lies after the body's (`PendingBody::specOrder`) is refused where the body uses it. Bodies made from templates are not checked (compiled after the unit, as cl instantiates them). cppparse instantiates a class when it is named, the standard when it is used: what the members of a merely named instantiation need waits on that class (`Symbol::needsOnUse`, `m_instantiating`), and a specialisation after a mere naming takes the instantiation's symbol over (discardInstantiation), so `A<int>*`, a typedef or a parameter declared before mean the specialisation, and the instantiations that were named with it and need it (`W<int>` with an `A<int>` member, a member typedef read through it) are declared again in their symbols once it is defined (reinstantiateDependents, instantiateClassFor; a specialisation only declared after such a naming is S132). The static data member's specialisation is its definition now (a strong symbol, as cl's; without an initialiser a declaration, 14.7.3/15). `examples/sample-front/explspec-used.cpp` (rejected, the eight lines cl and clang refuse) and `front2.cpp` `explorder::` (against cl at -O0 and -O2). | G1 | (rows 21 and B6's left items, gap analysis item 1) `struct S { ~S(); static void* operator new[](size_t); static void operator delete[](void*); }; S (*p)[3] = new S[2][3]; delete[] p;` was S132 twice, `new (arena) T[n]` with `operator new[](size_t, Arena&)` S132 "a placement new of an array with arguments other than an address", and `new (buf) C` for a class with its own `operator new(size_t, void*)` S132 | fixed (2026-11-12), measured on cl's code and output: a multidimensional array is one array of its innermost elements: `new T[n][3]` allocates n * 3 elements, the cookie holds that number (it held n; a mixed link with cl's delete[] destroyed too few), the class's own `operator new[]` gets n * 3 * sizeof(T) + 8, one loop constructs, and `delete[]` of a `T (*)[3]` destroys the cookie's count of T and frees through the class's `operator delete[]` (the sized form gets the whole size, one element's size without a cookie; a virtual destructor's vector deleting destructor as for one dimension) (IrGen `Expr::New`, `Expr::Delete`). `new A3` for `typedef T A3[3]` allocated one T without a cookie, which `delete[]` then read: it is `new T[3]` now (Sema::exprNew leaves the array type in `semaOperand`). Placement: Sema::exprNew resolves `operator new[]` like `operator new`, the class's own first (12.5), for every placement new; one address that selects the reserved form `operator new(size_t, void*)` (or nothing, when `<new>` is not included) stays the address itself, so `new (&pool) T` with a user's `operator new(size_t, Pool*)` calls that operator (it built the object in the pool object before) and `new (buf) C` calls C's own; a class that declares only other forms is S107, as cl's C2660. An array's operator new[] gets the size IrGen computes, cookie included as cl (`new (arena) D[3]` asks for 3 * sizeof(D) + 8 and yields the address + 8), in place of the placeholder sema passes; when an element's constructor throws, the elements built are destroyed and the matching placement `operator delete[]` gets the address the operator returned and the same arguments (IrGen::placementNew with sizeTemp, matchingPlacementDelete by the operator's name). A null result of a placement operator new or new[] builds nothing and is the value of the new-expression (5.3.4/13; `new ("x") Z` with a `throw()` operator returning 0 ran the constructor on a null pointer before). front2.cpp `newarr::` against cl at -O0 and -O2 (both EH models by hand); new-refused.cpp now holds three placement new-expressions that name no operator (S107) |

Found with it: row G17 |
| G17 | (found by row G11's probes) a program that defines a replaceable allocation function of its own (18.4.1.1/2: `void* operator new(size_t)` and `void operator delete(void*)`, to count or to pool) and links libcpp/iostream.cpp, as every program that uses `<iostream>` does, does not link: `error LNK2005: "void * __cdecl operator new(unsigned __int64)" (??2@YAPEAX_K@Z) already defined`, and the same for `operator delete`. libcpp's eight functions are ordinary definitions in an object that is always linked. Without libcpp the program links and its functions are called by `new`, `new[]` and `new (std::nothrow)` as in cl's build ("replaced 3 3" in both): the C runtime's functions lie one to an object in its libraries, so the linker takes only those the program leaves undefined | fixed (2026-11-18): libcpp's eight functions are defaults, defined as weak externals. `__declspec(replaceable)` on a function (cppparse's own attribute; libcpp/iostream.cpp writes it through a macro that is empty for cl) makes the back end define the code under `<name>$default` and the COFF writer emit the function's name as a weak external (`IMAGE_SYM_CLASS_WEAK_EXTERNAL`, a `;@weak <name> <default>` line of the listing) whose auxiliary record names that code with `IMAGE_WEAK_EXTERN_SEARCH_ALIAS`. Measured with link.exe on objects written for the purpose: with that characteristic the default stands for the name before the libraries are searched and gives way to a definition in any object, whatever the order of the objects; with `SEARCH_LIBRARY`, which is what an `/alternatename:` directive gives, the C runtime's function is linked in place of libcpp's, and with `SEARCH_NOLIBRARY` the name stays unresolved. So no `.drectve` section was needed, and no link command changes, which a library of one object to a function would have asked of every script and of the IDE. Each of the eight is replaced by itself. The ones the program leaves call the ones it defines, as the C runtime's do: `operator new[]` and the nothrow forms call `operator new` (row G11), and now `operator delete[]` and the nothrow deallocation functions call `operator delete` (they called `free`), so a program that defines `operator new` and `operator delete` alone has replaced all eight. The calls inside libcpp go through the names, and the optimiser does not inline a replaceable function (its body is only the default). `examples/sample-lib/replacenew.cpp` (its own `operator new` and `operator delete`, counting calls and bytes over `new`, `new[]`, an array with a count, `new (std::nothrow)`, the functions called by name, and a failing `operator new`: `bad_alloc` from the plain forms, null from the nothrow ones), `replacepart.cpp` (`operator new` alone) and `replacearray.cpp` (`operator new[]` and `operator delete[]` alone), all with `<iostream>`, in test 15 against cl's build with its own library at -O0 and -O2; `newhandler.cpp` is unchanged. cl's build of libcpp (mix.ps1) keeps plain definitions. Found with it: row G20 |
